The following gives the exact compact scalar argument and translates the central mechanisms of A/B/C, with the deep native input theorems explicitly separated. A proof body in the source is not by itself our human proof of it. No compact Lean build performed in this session.

Latest integration: the appendices now include the full theta-chain height calculation, a conjugate-pairing correction to the archimedean determinant argument, exact p-adic comparison coordinates, and the general-length tight-frame construction used by the local jet caller. These are human derivations with stated upstream inputs, pending independent audit. The historical OPEN lists below must be read with their later caller integrations; none is a claim that the full prime-error inequality or human RH proof is complete.

## 1. The central inequality

Fix Parameters P and admissible Data D as in the compact source. Write x,x1,b,a for scales, m for number of lifts, k for column rank, f for the number of archimedean places, and d_R=dim_C(rows)>0. Distinguish d_R from the quadratic-field prime d. Let
  B = f a (log b)^3,
  L = (log b) B,
  H = m k dirCost,
  R = remainder.
Use the exact source definitions of mainHeight, mainAsk and remainder. The central theorem is
  [star] m R <= (log b)^2 B.
It is proved by comparing upper and lower volume bounds for the SAME independent family of rows, at the SAME D and all the required embeddings/twists. No independent optimization of either bound.

## 2. Theorem A: short forms — normalization and height arithmetic

Let T be the actual finite set of non-split primes plus the level prime N. For a prime l put g_l=floor(allowedLoss(l) - 1_{non-split}(l) gain(l)), zero off T. A single rational number q=prod_{l in T} l^{g_l}>0 satisfies log q=sum g_l log l and |q|_l=l^{-g_l}. This simultaneously controls the size at infinity and the local divisibility at all primes; there is no independent local choice of q. For the native projected Poincare maps P_s, put B_s=q P_s. These are exactly the maps chosen in Data.short_forms.

The source's height computation has a separate simple cancellation. Write wbar=mean log(nominalWeight), G=log(gProd rootDisc/(2pi)), and ell=chowlaSelbergConst. The definition of mainAsk is a(log a-1-ell-wbar). The source jet-size formula and Stirling give
  jetSize(a)-mainAsk <= a(wbar+G)+1+(log a)/2.
The level-prime choice gives
  log N >= m(wbar+G) - G -(15/2)log b.
The exact heightPerRow identity is
  heightPerRow = H-mR + #tops [m(jetSize(a)-mainAsk)-a log N].
Substitute both inequalities BEFORE bounding terms. The common m a(wbar+G) cancels. Hence
  heightPerRow <= H-mR + #tops [m(1+(log a)/2)+a(G+(15/2)log b)]
              <= H-mR+B,
where the last inequality is the native HasLogBound scale estimate.

The deep remaining A inputs are the native seesaw identity, spanning by short ramified columns, the norm estimate of the composite lift, the uniform local index-divisibility supplier exists_indexDvd_Bmu_divisible, and sum_allowedLoss_mul_log_le, together with their precise prime/twist and scale guards. Given these, rational normalization and summing the local index bounds give the native A output:
- a spanning set S of integral ramified columns, such that ||Theta_1 B_s phi||_sigma^2 <= exp(h), h=H-mR+L, for all phi in S, s and sigma;
- any finite row family in the images of integral ramified columns under B_s for s in t has IndexLe at most |t| dim(columns) B.
The local bound supplies the needed integer exponent because -g_l=ceil(1_{non-split} gain-allowedLoss), strictly below that quantity plus 1. All twist-coprimality conditions in IndexLe remain. This paragraph is the native A assembly, not yet a human derivation of its deep inputs.

## 3. Theorem B: enough forms — direct rank-deficit proof of the greedy lemma

Here is a compact human derivation of the actual abstract exists_few_translates from New/Heights/OneDeterminant. Let V,W be the finite-dimensional column and row spaces, v=dim V>0, d_R=dim W>0, and let B_s:V->W satisfy IsSpread(B,eta), eta=b^{-2}>0: for every subspace F of W, some s satisfies
  dim B_s^{-1}(F) <= [(1+dim F/d_R)/2+eta]v.
The native filling theorem gives exactly this property on the full column space using its ramifying operator; its maps have the same ranges as the projected Poincare maps on ramified columns (surjectivity onto ramified columns must be kept). Nonzero scalar normalization q does not change those ranges.

For a selected set t let F_t=sum_{s in t} range B_s and r=d_R-dim F_t. By rank-nullity for V -> W/F_t, if r>4eta d_R there exists s whose new dimension g satisfies
  g >= v(r/(2d_R)-eta)>0.
Thus s was not already selected and r drops to r-g. Define a nonnegative potential
  P(r)=5 d_R log(1+r/(eta d_R)).
For r>4eta d_R,
  P(r)-P(r-g) >= 5 d_R g/(eta d_R+r)
                >= v * 5(r/2-eta d_R)/(r+eta d_R) >= v.
The first inequality is log(1-u)<=-u applied to u=g/(r+eta d_R)<1. The last follows from 3r/2>=6eta d_R. Start at r=d_R, and select such s until r<=4eta d_R; termination follows because r is a nonnegative integer strictly decreasing. If the starting r already meets the threshold choose t empty. Telescoping proves
  |t| v <= 5 d_R log(1+eta^{-1}),
  dim F_t >= (1-4eta)d_R.
No basis or translate multiplicity is counted twice. This is an elementary human proof of the native constant 5 and deficit 4, not a new asymptotic estimate. For W=0 the native abstract lemma is immediate with empty t; actual B uses positive d_R.

The actual missing B supplier is Data.ramifiedColumns_fill_rows, whose body invokes Fits.exists_translate_finrank_comap_le, Petersson second moment, central projection, bad_share_le and ramifyingOp_finrank_comap_and_short. Its positivity proof also gives finrank_rows_ne_zero. Translate these actual named inputs rather than assume that the Poincare family spans or is a tight frame without the native error. FillingTheorem.lean is already retrieved; its deeper moment and local rank proofs still require human reconstruction.

### Finite Fourier determinant and the global sieve (integrated peer derivations)

For the finite determinant bound, let G be finite, pi:G→GL_r(F_q), A_0,A_1 be r-by-h matrices and Theta(s)≥0 be positive type, with Gram vectors u_a satisfying <u_a,u_b>=Theta(a^-1 b). Write M=sum_s Theta(s). Assume the Theta-mass of each fixer of A_0 v and each dual fixer of A_1 y is ≤Y for v,y nonzero (the exact native fixer hypotheses). Let e_x be the standard basis indexed by F_q^r and f_z(w)=q^(-r/2) psi(-z^T w) its orthonormal Fourier basis. For nonzero y,v take U_y=sum_a f_(pi(a)^(-T) A_1 y) tensor u_a and V_v=sum_b e_(pi(b) A_0 v) tensor u_b. Both squared norms are ≤|G|Y, by grouping pairs by a^-1 b. Their inner product is exactly |G|q^(-r/2) sum_s Theta(s) psi(y^T A_1^T pi(s) A_0 v). Thus this character sum has modulus ≤q^(r/2)Y. Averaging over v and summing over y counts the left kernel of A_1^T pi(s) A_0, which has ≥q elements when the determinant vanishes. Terms with y=0 or v=0 contribute at most 2M in total, and the others at most q^(h+r/2)Y. Hence q mass(singular)≤2M+q^(h+r/2)Y, including h=0 trivially. The dual action is essential, not a second independent family. This proves the finite lemma used by Moments.sum_le_of_positive_type, from its fixer inputs.

The global sieve combines the local systems J_i, head bounds Lambda_i and frequency decay beta by product: Fubini gives the step integrals and tensor products of local Gram vectors give positivity of the top kernel. For common-entry heads, summing J_(i+1) is nonnegative by backwards induction: the top is a Gram quadratic form; the preceding common-entry sum is its next-entry integral. Fatou gives lower semicontinuity. These statements retain the native integrability and continuity premises. For prefix heads H_i(p,t) over A^i and a finite unit set V⊂A, let X_i(V)=Re sum_(t∈V,p,q∈A^i) J_(i+1)(H_i(p,t),H_i(q,t)). With native c_i=vol(O_hat)/prod_v frameScaleSq(v,i)^2 and C_i=prod_(a<i)c_a, the restricted-adele pullback has measure c_i times the local product measure. Outside the selected places T it is invariant by the omitted integral subgroup; a nonzero Fourier coefficient therefore forces its frequency to annihilate that subgroup. Self-duality of O_hat, after reindexing by the different generator, gives exactly the off-T integrality guard (not a unit condition). The native frequency and head-sum bounds give the error c_i epsilon Gamma C_i. One-sided Poisson gives
  X_i(V)≤c_i[Re sum_(p,q∈A^i) J_i(H_i(p,1),H_i(q,1))+epsilon Gamma C_i].
For i>0 the matching last entries in the double sum give exactly X_(i-1)(A), by the padding identity. Unequal last entries have nonzero off-T integral difference g^-1(t^-1-v^-1); the singleton frequency bound and head-sum bound control their total by epsilon Gamma C_i. Thus X_i(V)/C_(i+1)≤X_(i-1)(A)/C_i+2Gamma epsilon, with base X_0(V)/C_1≤En+Gamma epsilon. Induction gives X_i(V)≤C_(i+1)[En+(2i+1)Gamma epsilon]. This retains the actual quantitative local frequency/head bounds, real band limits, positive-type energy and finite-system witness; they do not follow merely from PSD.

## 4. A and B use one family; Theorem C uses one reciprocal identity

Since S spans the ramified columns, Y=union_{s in t} B_s(S) spans F_t. Choose an independent basis y of F_t from Y. Let n=|y|. Then (1-4/b²)d_R<=n<=d_R. A applies to this SAME y and t, giving some delta<=5 d_R log(1+b²)B with IndexLe(y,delta). For each sigma Hadamard's Gram determinant inequality gives covolumeSq(Theta_1 y)_sigma<=exp(n h), since every squared norm is <=exp(h). Thus take V=n h. This is the full finite-dimensional A+B assembly; it does not assume a full basis or use a different family for the lower bound.

C starts with ANY independent such y, IndexLe(y,delta), and all-conjugate squared-covolume bound exp(V). The source picks ONE fit-twist family theta indexed by p-adic embeddings. For its nonzero chain volumes z(theta) and z(1), the two source height estimates are
  HeightLe(V+2delta+d_R b, z(theta)/z(1)),
  HeightLe(2d_R(2L-H), z(1)^2/z(theta)^2).
They are not conventional nonnegative heights: native HeightLe allows signed upper budgets for embedding-dependent families. Its product rule holds by multiplying the same auxiliary numbers, adding archimedean exponents and adding finite-prime bounds. The product formula gives HeightLe(c,1) => c>=0. For the latter, the archimedean and p-adic bounds for the auxiliary nonzero number A imply the corresponding bounds for log absolute norm divided by the field degree, via all conjugate embeddings; sum the logarithmic rational product formula over a sufficiently large prime cutoff. The needed algebraicity of A and extension-of-embedding facts are from the actual HeightLe.nonneg body and remain referenced prerequisites.

Square the first ratio and multiply by the second ratio pointwise on the SAME theta:
  (z(theta)/z(1))^2 [z(1)^2/z(theta)^2]=1.
Therefore
  0 <= 2(V+2delta+d_R b)+2d_R(2L-H),
so V+2delta >= d_R(H-2L-b) >= d_R(H-3L), since b<=L by the retained size estimates. This proves the exact C scalar consequence from those two height inputs, with no absolute-value expansion of the reciprocal ratios.

The first height estimate uses a completion of y to a full basis by crude rows, the actual OneDeterminant theorem and the index matrices at conjugate p-adic embeddings. The Gram determinant identity det(Mbar G M^T)=det(Mbar)det(G)det(M) explains the two index contributions; the auxiliary rows cost d_R b in the source. Need reconstruct crude_rows, their size and integral pairing/denominator guards. The second height estimate uses the exact chain factorization z(v)=|c(v)|^{d_R} prod_j liftVolume(v,j), the fit family, and the individual lift ratio estimate HeightLe(d_R k[(log b)B0-dirCost],lambda_j(1)/lambda_j(theta)). Here m k B0=B. Sum over m lifts and square to obtain 2d_R(L-H); the constant ratio contributes at most 2d_R L. This gives the stated second bound. The deep remaining C suppliers are exists_isFitFamily (Selmer and Galois), volume_iota_eq_prod_liftVolume, heightLe_liftVolume_ratio (comparison operator, Hecke values, integral and archimedean determinant bounds), and the completion cost. They must be supplied for the identical D and theta; no independent optimizations. Actual source bodies of the immediate C estimates were read, but no native replay or full human reconstruction of these deep suppliers.

### C: the exact completion mechanism, including the two embeddings

Complete y to a basis by choosing a basis in the quotient of row space by span(y) from the images of the crude spanning family and lifting its members. Put kappa=d_R-n. Independence follows by projecting any putative relation to this quotient. For the same completed basis let G_v be the Gram matrix of its Theta_v images and A=det G_1≠0. The ratio det G_v/det G_1 is unchanged by a common basis change. At a p-adic embedding j, use two distinct index matrices, at j and at j composed with complex conjugation j'. Integral pairings give integrality of j(conj(M_j')) j(G_v) j(M_j)^T. Their determinant lower bounds are exp(-e_p)|j(gamma)|^kappa and exp(-e_p)|j'(gamma)|^kappa. Multiplying before taking norms gives |j(det G_v (gamma conj(gamma))^kappa)|≤exp(2e_p). No commutation of j and complex conjugation is assumed. With A as auxiliary number and sum e_p≤delta, the native HeightLe definition and multiplication by the reciprocal gamma family give the first row-volume estimate. The crude added rows each have squared norm ≤exp(x1^2 b^2), and gamma costs 2m² squares with per-square reciprocal height 2t x^999. Thus the pre-rounding height is V+2delta+kappa U with U=x1²b²+4m²t x^999. Multiplication of its square by the already stated reciprocal height gives d_R(H-2L)≤V+2delta+kappa U. With V=n(H-mR+L),
  n mR≤(n+2d_R)L+(d_R-n)(U-H)+2delta.
This optional exact signed budget retains the rank/height coupling, but the native rounded bound in §4 is sufficient, and no new estimate on U-H is needed to use the original proof. ### C: grid exchange to integral crude rows (new connected reconstruction, 26 November)

There are two grids V and Z, with diagonal Z_(j,j) obtained from V_(j,j) by adjoining one positive line (gridDiagEquiv_spec). At j=m both are definite. The source starts with one surjective complex-linear restriction/adjoint map r from gridLevelForms(m) onto principalForms(Y_0), independent of twist, and tidy data Λ_v of order 4m on path 0. The common period identity is index_v times the integral of Ξ against liftChainY_v(rφ)=α times gridPeriod(Λ_v;Ξ,φ), with α independent of v. Why surjective? RowsReached.eq_zero_of_orthogonal_gridLevelForms supplies one real point g with no nonzero principal row orthogonal to all bordered restrictions; GridFirstColumns.exists_restrict_bordered identifies their adjoints, and gridLevelForms_eq_map_iotaPlus gives the untwisted descent onto the initial grid space. The orthogonal complement of the range of their composite r is zero, so r is onto. The hard steep/Laplace assertion establishing that one g works for every row remains an explicit input, not a dimension assumption.

A tidy functional on the matrices along a path has two properties: covariance under the admissible node group with the product of edge characters, and expansion into ≤Q^n point evaluations with nonzero coefficients roots of unity and left-invertibility on the μ-edges (Q=tupleBound(v)). In GridMove.IsTidy.exists_square_move, replacing two edges by two edges has at most Q² choices. The partner graph kernel, multiplied by the Fourier character of the complementary block, transports the edge characters by its equivariance. Its auxiliary partner weight d(y) may be inserted or deleted by averaging over the free GL group at the old node: the exact average is 1 on the admissible source support. This is the equality G(1)=G(d) in the source, not a numerical approximation. For nonzero summands the partner lemma supplies root-of-unity coefficients and left inverses on the new μ-edges; hence the new functional is tidy of order n+2. Applying the partner identity pointwise to each original summand proves old period = C times new period, for functionals reading only the other unchanged edges. The actual square-law theorem and partner-kernel existence remain external to this paragraph; the transport and support mechanism are reconstructed from its body.

The row-ladder induction now gives the cost exactly. For a=0 the path is only the leg and there is nothing to exchange. In moving a descent past a+1 horizontal lifts, first use the induction for a lifts and then cross one square. The source one-square constant is κ/c(v), where κ is twist-independent and c costs one square. Multiplying the scalar factors adds the height costs, and the tidy order rises by 2 per move. This proves the descent_past_gridPathMap recurrence: cost a, order increment 2a, with other edges unchanged and the stated dependence-on-w guard. At diagonal j, perform this in both grids (cost j+j, order increment 4j), then the coupled diagonal square exchange (one cost square, two more tidy orders). The final Fourier normalization c_F has |c_F|² a fixed multiple of the residue-ring cardinality to a fixed integer power; CostsSquares.of_norm_sq_eq_mul_card_zpow makes its reciprocal height cost zero for the SAME fit family. A distinction matters: the native HasRoute uses the common bound 4j+2 both for tidy order and cost, which dominates the cost sum, rather than claiming that all 4j+2 terms are independent L-values. The seesaw identities transport the diagonal period; all scalar numerators independent of v are absorbed in its g-dependent constant, and the nonzero twist factor d_j(v) stays in the denominator. Thus if the new periods equal β_g T(v,Ξ,φ), the old equal β'_g T(v,Ξ,φ)/d_j(v). All choices precede T and g. The order increment and cost are ≤4j+2. Composing this identity over j gives d(v)=prod_(j<m)d_j(v) and total 2m² since sum_(j<m)(4j+2)=2m². In particular the final order is 4m+2m². This is the actual gridPeriod_climb, without independent routes for different input pairs. The one-square/real seesaw and partner-kernel supplier hypotheses are preserved.

At the last path the forms are chains of algebraic theta kernels on definite groups. GridFirstColumns.gridPeriod_eq_endPeriod proves by induction that each path map is a scalar independent of the form times the algebraic chain times the ratio of its end-level indices. When substituted into the diagonal period, the branching pairing and the quotient-measure indices cancel the twist-dependent level ratio, leaving a scalar β independent of v, Ξ and φ. This gives exactly the native class-integral endPeriod. Its bilinear form is v'^T B v with the chosen integer branching matrix B from endBranching (interlacing retained). The normalization is not an arbitrary comparison of two Haar measures.

There is one useful general finite-sum transport in GridFirstColumns.endPeriod_mem. Let S(z,j,g,w) be a predicate on each intermediate vector, true of the two initial forms and preserved by each endKernel step. Suppose every final pair satisfying S has |σ(M v'^T B v)|≤R. Covariance of Λ cancels the edge-character factors of the end kernels under changes of level representatives, so the class integrals become finite sums over the native standard classes, with no additional twist-dependent denominator. Expand the tidy functional as ≤Q^n point evaluations. Each chain is a sum over its intermediate classes; the common final class is counted once. Each coefficient has norm ≤1 under σ, since it is zero or a root of unity. Therefore endPeriod is a sum of at most Q^n prod_(z=0,1) prod_(j<m+1-z) #StdClasses(endLevel(v,z,j)) terms each satisfying |σ(M term)|≤R. This is exactly the nsmul assertion of endPeriod_mem, with n the tidy order, not row rank. For ultrametric σ and R=1, the number of summands is irrelevant: every finite sum of valuation-ring elements is integral. For a complex automorphism, the triangle inequality multiplies R by the displayed number of terms. These are two applications of the SAME expansion, not two unrelated spanning families.

The uniform denominator theorem applies the ultrametric version with a scaled Weyl gauge at step j, namely w↦weylGauge(w multiplied by ν^j)≤1, and the branching correction determined by the determinant exponents L_tau. The actual integer N is of the form prod_tau (gProd^b1 N1)^(m |L_tau|) times ν^(2m). The source obtains ν from the uniform vacuum/raising and weight bounds, not by clearing the denominators of finitely many chosen twists after the fact. The term count makes no contribution to N. In the archimedean version the trivial twist and the square-root Weyl norm are used with growth G^j, G=exp(x^99 b²); the same expansion and class-count bounds give exp(x^200 b²) sqrt(B_false B_true) for the end period. The explicit sufficient ν and the logarithmic budget are derived in §6g below from the native vacuum, jet, and branching interfaces; their underlying proof obligations remain separately identified.

Finally choose a short spanning set in the grid-level source, using its standard algebraic-form equivalence. Pair the endpoint lift with delta forms at each class and integral test vector; the dual-pairing matrix identifies these scalar pairings with its coordinates. The period route gives the same expression for all twists. With the common integer denominator N, the one scalar on the original row includes c(1), so multiplying its twisted lift by c(v)/c(1) precisely clears the route denominator c(v). The remaining period factor is β/β (zero or one), after an associated unit is extracted as in crude_rows; no assumption that a period β is nonzero is made. The coordinate identity in crude_rows then gives integrality for p coprime to the twist level norm. The untwisted coordinate bound follows using the same test pairing at complex conjugates σ, and the complex end-period bound; the source dual Weyl norm and hook-number estimates convert it to squared norm ≤exp(x1²b²). Since r is surjective and the common row scaling is nonzero, the scaled images of the short set span the principal rows. This is the supplier of the completion in §4, conditional on the still unexpanded local quantitative and duality lemmas. No RH premise.

## 5. The common main term cancels

Use the crude bound |H|+m|R|<=b² x^99 from native abs_mainHeight_add_remainder_le and L>=0, hence -h<=b² x^99. Apply C to the y of §4 and V=n h:
  d_R(H-3L)<= n h +2delta
    = d_R h+(d_R-n)(-h)+2delta
    <= d_R[h+4x^99+10 log(1+b²)B].
The missing dimension term uses 0<=d_R-n<=4d_R/b²; signs matter if h is negative. Divide by d_R>0 and substitute h=H-mR+L. H cancels:
  mR <= 4L+4x^99+10log(1+b²)B <= (log b)^2 B.
Last inequality is native HasLogBound, not an arbitrary numerical assumption. This is the exact compact star_inequality. No conditional deep input has been deleted.

## 6. From star to RH: keep the compact route, not the old G window

For the compact R_M(x)=primeError(d,M,x), lambda_M(n)=Lambda(n)(1-chi_d(n)) when n=1 mod M and zero otherwise. R_M(x)=sum_{n<=x}lambda_M(n)(1/n-1/x)-log x/phi(M)-Re logDerivMean(d,M,1). The actual prime-sum identity is
  R=a[#thick(R_N0(x)-R_N0(x1))+#tops R_1(x1)].
Each top translation contributes all thick places when the class condition holds, none otherwise. The main log x/log x1 and Chowla-Selberg constants cancel by the actual nominal-weight average and f=phi(N0)#tops. See remainder_eq. This is not the old G(x)/x.

For fixed P and 0<epsilon<1/100, exists_data gives D at every sufficiently large x with log b=x^epsilon, log x1=x^(epsilon/6), (10^6 log x)^2<=log x1, #thick>=#tops/log b and m>=sqrt x. Let t=10^6 log x. The actual IsPolar.exists_window_sub_mul_le supplies u in [t,t²] with
  R_N0(exp u) - (#tops/#thick) R_1(exp u)<=exp(-t)
because the ratio is nonnegative and at most exp(t). The native D' changes only logX1 to u and proves its scale condition, preserving the other data. Apply star on D':
  R_N0(x)<= f(log b)^5/(m #thick)+x^(-10^6)
          <= phi(N0) x^(6epsilon-1/2)+x^(-10^6)
          <= x^(8epsilon-1/2) eventually.
Here the eventual threshold may depend on P and epsilon. All scales and their existence are retained; no intersection of sparse windows is taken.

The native analytic criterion uses the polar Laplace transform of R_M(exp v) and its residue at s, given by (sum_xi [ord_s Ltilde(xi)-ord_s Ltilde(xi chi_d)])/[phi(M)s(1-s)]. It selects a prime d in the specified unit class whose quadratic twists do not vanish at the relevant ordinate, then applies its Landau lemma. At modulus q lift to N0=18480 q and class N0/2-1; for a hypothetical zero with Re rho>1/2 choose epsilon=(min(Re rho,1)-1/2)/51, so 0<epsilon<1/100 and 1/2+8epsilon<Re rho. This leads to New.grh and, at modulus 1, the functional equation gives RH. The compact analytic suppliers (polar identity, nonvanishing twist, good second scale, Landau and DataExist) are not independently fully derived here; they are explicit required modules for the editor and analytic lane. Do not claim our earlier old-pin conditional Landau replay verifies these compact declarations.

## 6a. Direct second-scale elimination by the classical fixed-modulus prime number theorem (interactive)

This is a human-proof shortcut replacing the special high-moment selection of the second scale, not a new RH criterion. Keep P=(N0,d,...) FIXED while x grows, as in exists_data. We use the unconditional classical prime number theorem for each fixed progression (e.g. Davenport, Multiplicative Number Theory, ch. 20; Montgomery–Vaughan, Multiplicative Number Theory I, ch. 11; explicit form in A. Strömbergsson, analytic number theory notes, Thm 15.1, https://www2.math.uu.se/~ast10761/analtalt08/www_notes.pdf). For fixed modulus Q and unit residue class a it gives psi(y;Q,a)=y/phi(Q)+O_Q(y exp(-c_Q sqrt(log y))) for some c_Q>0. A possible exceptional real zero beta_Q<1 contributes O_Q(y^beta_Q), absorbed in this estimate for fixed Q. No RH/GRH hypothesis and no uniformity in growing moduli is required. The theorem is a conventional external cited result for the human proof; this shortcut has not been replayed in compact Lean.

Fix either M=N0 or M=1 and put Q=Md, coprime M,d. Let lambda_M(n) denote the actual weighted Mangoldt function of PrimeError, and A_M(y)=sum_{n<=y}lambda_M(n). Split into the reduced classes mod Q. The nonzero character chi_d sums to zero over units modulo d; CRT gives sum_{a mod Q, (a,Q)=1, a=1 mod M}(1-chi_d(a))=phi(d). The nonunit terms occur only at powers of the finitely many primes dividing Q and contribute O_Q(log y) (and M divisors are excluded by the class condition). Thus the fixed-modulus theorem yields A_M(y)=y/phi(M)+E_M(y), with |E_M(y)|<=C y exp(-c sqrt(log y)) for y>=2; enlarge C on [1,2]. All constants may depend on the fixed P. The same c can be used for M=1,N0 by taking their minimum.

The exact ramp sum is
  sum_{n<=y}lambda_M(n)(1/n-1/y)=integral_1^y A_M(v)/v² dv.
This follows by integrating each summand over v>=n (lambda_M(1)=0). Therefore the ramp minus log y/phi(M) tends to K_M=integral_1^infty E_M(v)/v² dv. Identify this constant, rather than silently choose a new normalization. For real s>1, by absolute convergence and the character Euler products used in native PrimeError,
  F_M(s)=sum_{n>=1}lambda_M(n)n^-s
        = [1/(s-1)+sum_xi(logDeriv(entireL(quadTwist xi d))(s)-logDeriv(entireL(xi))(s))]/phi(M)
        = s/[phi(M)(s-1)]+logDerivMean(d,M,s).
Partial summation also gives
  F_M(s)=s integral_1^infty A_M(v)v^(-s-1)dv
        = s/[phi(M)(s-1)]+s integral_1^infty E_M(v)v^(-s-1)dv.
Subtract the same singular term and take s down to 1. Dominated convergence follows from the integrable exponential-square-root bound; entireL for the principal character is regularized by (s-1), and every relevant entireL is nonzero at 1 (native PolarLandau.entireL_ne_zero). Hence K_M=Re logDerivMean(d,M,1), the exact native normalization. In particular
  R_M(y)= -integral_y^infty E_M(v)/v² dv,
  |R_M(y)|<= 2 C (sqrt(log y)/c+1/c²) exp(-c sqrt(log y)),
using u=sqrt(log v), dv/v=2u du. This proves the claimed error without any unknown constant offset.

Now use the ORIGINAL D supplied by exists_data, NOT D' with logX1 replaced. It has log x1=x^(epsilon/6), log b=x^epsilon, #tops/#thick<=log b=x^epsilon, m>=sqrt x. Apply star and the exact remainder_eq to this D:
  R_N0(x)<= f(log b)^5/(m #thick)+R_N0(x1)-(#tops/#thick)R_1(x1)
         <= phi(N0) x^(6epsilon-1/2)+(1+x^epsilon) O_P((1+x^(epsilon/12)) exp(-c x^(epsilon/12))).
For every fixed epsilon>0, the final term is O_P,epsilon(x^-A) for every fixed A>0 (x^(epsilon/12)/log x tends to infinity). Take A=1, absorb the fixed phi(N0) and error into x^(2epsilon), and recover exactly the native eventual bound R_N0(x)<=x^(8epsilon-1/2).

This removes the need, in the human proof, to reconstruct IsPolar.eventually_moment_le, IsPolar.exists_window_sub_mul_le, the special D' scale substitution, or its quantitative moment machinery. It does NOT remove the polar Mellin identity for the final Landau criterion, the zero-density/nonvanishing quadratic-twist theorem, DataExist, or A/B/C. The classical fixed-modulus theorem is unconditional and does not depend on the RH being proved. The order of quantifiers is essential: fix P before x→∞, including d; do not apply this argument if d or N0 is allowed to grow with x. The peer RUN50 fixed-modulus reduction (284991cdd00955a2c72a6421a0ae5bc25eba1b20) is compatible; the peer linear-log window (3e627c9ba1a8367044293f16015bde7e656ba0da) is a fallback rather than needed for this human route. No claim of a new general PNT theorem.

## 6b. Complete human analytic endgame from the native star bound and data existence

26 November 2026, interactive derivation. This section replaces the analytic gaps listed in the previous draft: exact polar transform, quadratic-twist nonvanishing selection and Landau implication. It uses conventional Pólya–Vinogradov, Poisson summation for a primitive Dirichlet character, Hadamard three-lines, the classical fixed-modulus PNT and the standard analytic continuation/functional equation of Dirichlet L-functions. These are unconditional classical theorems; no RH/GRH premise. The deep compact geometric star inequality and admissible data existence remain necessary. The derivation follows the actual compact source, with the fixed-modulus PNT shortcut of §6a; no claim of independently compiling it.

### Weighted Mellin transform and residue

For fixed M,d in §6a put a_M=1/phi(M), K_M=Re logDerivMean(d,M,1), F_M(s)=sum lambda_M(n)n^-s. In Re s>1, Euler products give F_M(s)=s a_M/(s-1)+logDerivMean(d,M,s), with the same entireL regularization as the compact source. For Re s>2, absolute convergence permits integration term by term:
  integral_n^infinity (1/n-1/x)x^-s dx
       = n^-s/[s(s-1)].
Thus the transform of R_M is
  Phi_M(s)=integral_1^infinity R_M(x)x^-s dx
       = [logDerivMean(d,M,s)/s-K_M]/(s-1).
In logarithmic coordinates x=exp v this is exactly integral_0^infinity R_M(exp v) exp((1-s)v)dv. The expression on the right is meromorphic. The sum defining logDerivMean is real at real s>1 by conjugate pairing of characters (the twisting character is real), hence is real at 1 by continuity. All entireL are nonzero at 1, so the apparent pole at 1 is removable. At any rho with Re rho>0 and rho!=1, the logarithmic derivatives have simple poles, with residue the zero order. Therefore
  Res_rho Phi_M =
    [sum_xi ord_rho entireL(xi)-sum_xi ord_rho entireL(xi chi_d)]
       /[phi(M) rho(1-rho)].
EntireL denotes (s-1)L(s,xi) for principal xi and L(s,xi) otherwise. All relevant poles are at most simple. The classical zero-free line Re s>=1 (after principal regularization) holds unconditionally; no claim of zero-free critical strip. This proves the native IsPolar facts actually needed, with the crude O(exp v) bound coming from 0<=lambda(n)<=2n (or the sharper §6a).

### Quadratic-twist selection: short derivation of the actual density input

Fix a character xi modulo M, induced from primitive psi of conductor h|M, and fix eta>0 and height Y>=0. For primes d=7 mod 8, d prime to M, the twist psi*(./d) is primitive of conductor q=hd (CRT of coprime primitive factors) and nonprincipal. Its parity j in {0,1} depends only on psi, since (−1/d)=−1. The imprimitive twist xi*(./d) differs from this primitive L-function by finitely many Euler factors nonzero when Re s>0. Quadratic reciprocity, including (2/d)=1, gives (n/d)=J(−d|n), where J is the native Jacobi symbol with the principal-character convention at 2; for odd d this agrees at 2. This is the exact convention in native ZeroFreeTwist.

For g_j(u)=u^j exp(−pi u²), and |c(n)|<=1, define theta_{c,v}(t)=sum_{n>=1} c(n)J(−v|n)g_j(nt), and I_{c,v}(T)=integral_(1/T)^infinity |theta_{c,v}(t)| dt/sqrt(t). We show, for X<=T² and t>=1/T>0,
  sum_{v<=X}|theta_{c,v}(t)|² <= C_eta T² t^(-1-2eta).
Expand the square (absolute convergence of the Gaussian series) and combine the Jacobi symbols into J(−v|nm). For pairs for which knm is square for k=1 or 2, use the trivial bound X on the sum over v and |g_j(nt)|<=C_eta (nt)^(-1/2-eta). In this square sector write kn=a b², m=a u², a squarefree, b,u positive. The product of the majorants sums to at most a constant times
  t^(-1-2eta) sum_{k=1,2} k^(1/2+eta)
    sum_{a,b,u>=1} (abu)^(-1-2eta),
which converges. (Dropping squarefree and divisibility restrictions only enlarges the positive sum.) For every other pair, the Jacobi symbol as a character of v is nonprincipal: some odd prime in nm has odd valuation. Pólya–Vinogradov for the possibly imprimitive character bounds the sum over v by 2 sqrt(nm)(1+log(nm)), as in the native lemma. With |g_j(nt)|<=C_eta (nt)^(-3/2-eta), the remaining double sum is bounded by a constant times t^(-3-2eta) times sum_{n,m}(nm)^(-1-eta)(1+log(nm)), finite. Since t^-2<=T², this proves the displayed estimate uniformly in c. The Gaussian bounds follow because u^A g_j(u) is bounded for every A>0.

Cauchy–Schwarz in v and integration give
  sum_{v<=X} I_{c,v}(T) <= C_eta T² integral_(1/T)^infinity t^(-1-eta)dt
                           = O_eta(T^(2+eta)).
Use this for c=psi and c=mu*psi; both have modulus<=1. This is the native sum_thetaTailIntegral_le in human form.

Now let chi be any primitive nonprincipal character of parity j, conductor q<=T², and suppose the sum of its two tails (with coefficients chi and mu*chi) is <=T^(2eta). Define the theta functions theta_chi(t) and theta_muchi(t) using g_j, and the Mellin gamma factor G_j(s)=pi^(-(s+j)/2)Gamma((s+j)/2)/2, which is nonzero for Re s>0. Poisson summation for the primitive character gives |theta_chi(t)|=(sqrt(q)t)^-1 |theta_chi(1/(qt))|; conjugation does not change the norm because g_j is real. Under t->1/(qt), the measure |theta_chi(t)|dt/sqrt(t) is transported exactly to the same measure. Since q<=T², the small range t<1/T maps inside t'>1/T. Hence the full theta_chi integral on the critical line is at most twice its tail.

Define the holomorphic bounded function on the strip 1/2<=Re s<=2
  H_chi(s)=G_j(s)^2 - G_j(s)L(s,chi) J_chi(s),
  J_chi(s)=integral_(1/T)^infinity theta_muchi(t)t^(s-1)dt.
The Mellin identity G_j L=integral_0^infinity theta_chi(t)t^(s-1)dt holds on Re s>0 by Poisson summation and analytic continuation of the absolutely convergent identity for Re s>1. The functions are bounded on this strip for each fixed chi,T, by the Gaussian decay at infinity and at zero for the primitive nonprincipal theta, and the cutoff for J_chi. On Re s=1/2 the tail hypothesis and the preceding norm identity give |G_j L|<=2T^(2eta), |J_chi|<=T^(2eta); G_j is bounded uniformly on the strip by its defining integral. Therefore |H_chi|<=C T^(4eta). On Re s=2, the absolutely convergent Dirichlet series of mu*chi is 1/L(s,chi); subtracting its truncated Mellin transform gives
  H_chi(s)=G_j(s)L(s,chi) integral_0^(1/T) theta_muchi(t)t^(s-1)dt.
Use |theta_muchi(t)|<=3e^-t/t, from summing the Gaussian majorant, and |L(2+i tau,chi)|<=zeta(2). Thus |H_chi|<=C/T, in particular <=C T^(4eta-1) for T>=1. Hadamard three-lines gives, at sigma=Re s,
  |H_chi(s)|<=C T^[4eta-(sigma-1/2)/(3/2)].
At a zero with sigma>=1/2+9eta this is at most C T^(-2eta). But there H_chi(s)=G_j(s)^2, whose norm has a positive lower bound on the compact rectangle 1/2<=sigma<=1, |Im s|<=Y, independently of chi. For large T this is impossible. Hence any such zero requires the combined two tails to exceed T^(2eta).

For primes d<=X in the required class take T=sqrt(hX). The two-tail sum estimate now gives at most O(T^(2+eta)/T^(2eta))=O_{M,eta,Y}(X^(1-eta/2)) bad primes for the fixed xi. Summing over the finite character group modulo M preserves the exponent. If beta>1/2 choose 0<9eta<min(1,beta-1/2). By the ordinary PNT in any fixed unit class a mod M, the number of primes in that class up to X is asymptotic to X/[phi(M)log X]; it exceeds the exceptional count for large X. Thus one prime d=a mod M simultaneously has all its twisted L-functions nonzero on Re s>=beta, |Im s|<=Y (Re s>=1 handled by the standard unconditional nonvanishing). All constants allowed to depend on M,beta,Y. This is the needed native exists_entireL_quadTwist_ne_zero; no estimate uniform in a growing modulus is required. In particular, ordinary RH only needs M=18480 and a=9239, a unit class with d=7 mod 8. Do not infer this simultaneous nonvanishing from Dirichlet's theorem alone.

### Landau and the final contradiction

For clarity the exact one-sided Landau principle used here can be proved directly. Suppose E(v) real, continuous or measurable with local integrability, |E(v)|=O(e^v), with its transform Phi(s)=integral_0^infinity E(v)e^((1-s)v)dv initially for Re s>2 and meromorphic on Re s>0 with at most simple poles, and suppose E(v)<=e^((beta-1)v) eventually for beta>0. If residues of Phi at real a>beta are nonnegative, Phi has no poles in Re s>beta. Indeed choose K>=1 so K e^(beta v)-E(v)e^v is nonnegative for all v>=0 (our E is continuous). Its Laplace transform is Psi(s)=K/(s-beta)-Phi(s). A nonnegative Laplace integral has a real singularity at any finite abscissa of convergence a: if analytic across a, choose c>a close enough to a that the Taylor series at c converges at some c-h<a. Its coefficients after replacing s by c-h are nonnegative moments integral v^j e^-cv [density] dv /j!, justified by exponential convergence on a neighborhood of c. Monotone convergence then sums to the integral at c-h, finite, contradicting the abscissa. If a>beta, meromorphic Psi has at most a simple pole there, of residue minus Res_a Phi<=0. A negative residue is impossible because Psi(s)>=0 for real s>a; a zero residue makes the singularity removable. Therefore a<=beta (including the possibility of convergence everywhere); the nonnegative Laplace integral is holomorphic on Re s>beta by dominated convergence on smaller right half-planes. Its analytic continuation identity with Psi gives the result. This is the ordinary Landau argument, not the compact special-window moment theorem.

Assume a nontrivial zeta zero rho with sigma=Re rho>1/2; classical nonvanishing for Re s>=1 gives sigma<1. Choose 1/2<beta<sigma and epsilon>0 with epsilon<1/100, 1/2+8epsilon<=beta. Fix M=18480, a=9239. By the density argument choose a prime d=a mod M simultaneously good for every twist at Re s>=beta, |Im s|<=|Im rho|. This d is fixed BEFORE letting x tend to infinity. It meets the actual Parameters guards of New.grh for q=1 (a²=1 mod M, gcd(a,M)=1, a=7 mod 8). By the compact geometric star inequality, data existence and §6a, E(v)=R_M(exp v)<=e^((beta-1)v) eventually. All real poles a' in (beta,1) of its transform have nonnegative residues, because the twisted zero orders vanish and the denominator phi(M)a'(1-a') is positive. There are no poles on [1,infinity). Apply Landau. At rho the induced principal character mod M has L(s,1_M)=zeta(s)prod_{p|M}(1-p^-s), whose Euler factors are nonzero in Re s>0. Thus its entireL has a positive zero order at rho; all other untwisted orders are nonnegative and all twisted orders vanish. The residue formula gives a nonzero residue, contradicting Landau. So no nontrivial zeta zero lies right of 1/2; the functional equation reflects every left-hand zero to the right, yielding RH.

This completes the human analytic IMPLICATION from the actual compact star inequality and admissible data existence using conventional mathematical results. It removes the prior special-scale moment construction and translates the quadratic-twist and Landau suppliers in full, rather than leaving the latter as unexamined black boxes. It does not establish the deep A/B/C geometric and arithmetic arguments or prove DataExist. Native replay not performed. No claim of a new result on RH itself or novelty of the classical analytic estimates.

## 6c. Admissible data existence: direct construction with classical Siegel–Walfisz

26 November 2026. The following is a human proof of the actual DataExist.exists_data statement using the exact Data record and a conventional external theorem. The auxiliary prime-window moment argument is unnecessary in this human route. No RH/GRH assumption. The constants can be ineffective because the theorem only asserts eventual existence for fixed P and epsilon. Native replay outstanding.

Fix the native Parameters P (including the quadratic prime d and modulus N0) and 0<epsilon<1/100. Write x for the physical scale and l=log x. Choose
  logX=l, logB=x^epsilon, logX1=x^(epsilon/6), b=exp(logB), x1=exp(logX1),
  m=2^ceil(log_2 sqrt(x)), r=3^ceil(log_3 x^5), s=22 log_2 m.
For x sufficiently large the integer exponents are positive. Thus sqrt(x)<=m<2sqrt(x), x^5<=r<3x^5, m and r have the required forms 2^(o+1), 3^(e+1), and
  s<=11 l/log 2+22 <=32l.
Also x^11<=2^s=m^22<2^22 x^11. The five Data.scale conditions follow from epsilon l->infinity, 100epsilon<1, x^(epsilon/6)/l->infinity, x^(5epsilon/6)->infinity and the exact equality 6 log logX1=log logB. In detail: 10^12 P.c<=epsilon l eventually; 100epsilon l<=l; 10^6 l<=logX1; 10^6 logX1<=logB. The extra (10^6 log x)^2<=logX1 also holds eventually. These choices keep every native magnitude lemma available for this D.

Let c=floor(log_3 s)+5, H=N0*3^c, N1=H*2^s, towerDeg=3^c*2^s, and L=324s. For fixed P, H=Theta_P(s) and towerDeg=Theta_P(s 2^s). The prime factors of N1 are exactly those of N0 (N0 is divisible by 18480). Infinite places are identified with the units modulo N1 via native Parameters.placeMulEquiv; phi is reduction to units modulo 2^(s+1), a divisor of N1. Put g=placeOfInt(s,H+1) and c0=placeOfInt(s,-1). The integer H+1 is prime to N1. Write v=v_2(H)=v_2(N0)>=4, since 18480 is divisible by 16. The order J of H+1 modulo 2^(s+1) is exactly 2^(s+1-v) for s+1>v. Indeed for an odd u=1+H with v_2(u-1)>=2, squaring raises v_2(u-1) by exactly one: u+1 has valuation 1 and u²-1=(u-1)(u+1). For an odd exponent the valuation remains unchanged by factoring the geometric sum, which is odd. This establishes the exact order, not merely a lower bound. Hence J=Theta_P(2^s).

Choose a FIXED integer n>=3 with 22/n<epsilon (e.g. n>44/epsilon). Let B=floor(J^(1/n)/L), a positive integer tending to infinity. This B is a digit bound, not Data.b or the error budget. Then (LB)^n<=J and B>=J^(1/n)/(2L) eventually. Among the B^n lattice points u=(u_1,...,u_n) with coordinates in {0,...,B-1}, some sphere of constant squared Euclidean norm contains at least B^n/[n(B-1)^2+1]>=B^(n-2)/n points. Use such a sphere S. Encode u by e(u)=sum_{a=0}^{n-1}u_(a+1)(LB)^a, and put
  T={c0*g^e(u):u in S}.
The map is injective even after applying phi, since exponents are distinct in [0,(LB)^n), which lies in [0,J). Every T element is congruent to -1 modulo H, because g is congruent to 1 modulo H. Therefore the entire native thick_eq_neg_one_mod field holds for every divisor of H that divides N1.

To check the native three_term field, suppose sigma,tau,rho in T correspond to u,v,w in S, and phi(tau/sigma)^j=phi(rho/sigma)^i with 0<i<j<=L. This means j e(v)=i e(w)+(j-i)e(u) modulo J. Each side has base-LB digits between zero and j(B-1)<LB, so the encoded integers are both in [0,(LB)^n)<=J. Equality modulo J is therefore ordinary equality, without carry. Coordinatewise jv=iw+(j-i)u, so v is a strict convex combination of u,w. The three points have the same squared norm; strict convexity of the squared Euclidean norm implies u=w=v. In particular phi(tau/sigma)=1 as required. This argument proves the native Behrend supplier in the exact needed setting.

The thickness count is
  |T| >= B^(n-2)/n
      >= J^(1-2/n)/[n(2L)^(n-2)].
Since J=Theta_P(2^s), towerDeg=Theta(s 2^s), L=324s and 2^s=Theta(x^11), we obtain
  towerDeg/|T| <= C_(P,n) s^(n-1) x^(22/n)=o(x^epsilon)=o(logB).
This uses the fixed strict inequality 22/n<epsilon, since fixed powers of log x are subpolynomial. Thus |T|>=towerDeg/logB for every sufficiently large x. The selected n and digit bound are not required Data fields; this legitimate alternative witness replaces the native elaborate rounding of W and the digit base.

It remains to supply the two special primes, their norm generator, and every prime window. Put Q=Momega(s)^2=(N0*d)^(2(s+6)), the actual native modulus, and W=x^(epsilon/6)=sqrt[6](logB). Since s<=32 log x, Q<=W^A for some FIXED A>0 depending only on P,epsilon (choose A> (6/epsilon)*70 log(N0*d), enlarging threshold). N1*d divides Momega by the native arithmetic identity N1*d|Momega; it also follows directly because 3^c*2^s divides (N0*d)^(s+5), using floor(log_3 s)<=s, and 6|N0. 

The standard unconditional Siegel–Walfisz theorem says for any fixed A and D>0, uniformly for q<=(log y)^A and gcd(a,q)=1,
  psi(y;q,a)=y/phi(q)+O_(A,D)(y/(log y)^D).
Equivalently use its exponential-square-root error version. Source: Dimitris Koukoulopoulos, The Distribution of Prime Numbers, AMS 2019, Theorem 12.1 (publisher-author preliminary chapter https://dms.umontreal.ca/~koukoulo/documents/publications/primes.pdf); Kedlaya, Analytic Number Theory notes, §10.11 https://kskedlaya.org/ant/chap-errorbounds2.html. No need for effective constants. For U>=W, let y=exp U. Then Q<=W^A<=U^A=(log y)^A. Apply the theorem at y and 2y, with D>A+2. Their main-term difference is y/phi(Q)>=y/U^A and their total error is O(y/U^D), uniformly for every U>=W, hence the difference is positive and >=y/(2U^A) for sufficiently large W. Terms at proper prime powers contribute at most O(sqrt(y)(log y)^2): there are at most sqrt(2y) possible bases and O(log y) exponents, each von Mangoldt weight <=log(2y). This is o(y/U^A) uniformly for U>=W as W->infinity. Consequently for every real U>=W there exists a prime p=1 mod Q with
  U<log p<=U+log 2<U+W.
This proves more than the actual required prime-window field, since every U in [100 logB,2^s] satisfies U>=W, and W<=sqrt(logB)=W^3. No varying-modulus fixed-PNT shortcut: the uniform Siegel–Walfisz theorem is used precisely where Q grows.

For the two g_i, let h_K be the class number of K=Q(sqrt(-d)), fixed with P. For i=0,...,h_K, use the prime-window result at U_i=10 logB+2i W. The intervals [U_i,U_i+W] are pairwise disjoint and contained in [10 logB,11 logB] for large x, since (2h_K+1)W<=W^6=logB. ### A. Local index and global rational normalization

A valuation-ring module inclusion has IndexDvd(M,N,z) if for EVERY finite family v_i in M a matrix u over the valuation ring satisfies det(u)|z and u v has each member in N. This is not an assumed finite-index cardinality, and works for arbitrary finite families (including dependent ones). In the actual New/Core/ModuleIndex.lean:
- Pullback along a linear map: apply the given matrix to the image of the same family, then use the stated implication for membership.
- Composition: if u sends the family to N, and v sends u's family to P, vu sends the original family to P; det(vu)=det(v)det(u) divides the product of the two allowed scalars.
- Pushforward modulo N': for each member of M'⊆f(M)+N' choose a preimage in M and an additive remainder in N'. Apply the original index matrix to the chosen preimages. The remainders stay in N' under any valuation-ring linear combination. This proves IndexDvd.map.
- For finitely many maps f_s from M, all with the same local determinant allowance z into N', start from the sum of their images and N'. Apply the preceding pushforward step to remove one image at a time, then the composition identity. The resulting single matrix has determinant dividing z^|t|. This proves IndexDvd.sup with no assumption that translates are independent. The number of selected rows n never replaces |t| in this exponent.
- For operators preserving M, HasType(p,M,j,T,c) means IndexDvd(M,TM+p^jM,p^l) for l<=c. The same composition and pushforward steps imply additivity of the exponent allowances for products of operators: if R preserves M, then R(p^jM)⊆p^jM, and applying R after a repair into TM+p^jM gives a repair into RTM+p^jM. Induction handles repeated powers and all finite ordered products. This is the exact HasType.prod mechanism. No commutation is necessary.

For a rational prime p put err_p=allowedLoss(p), G_p=gain(p) for nonsplit p<x1 and 0 otherwise, L_p=loss(p) for small nonsplit primes. Let beta_p=ceil(G_p-err_p). This is the integer in the actual A caller. All local gauge statements refer to the same good datum Psi for any twist of level prime to p, and all eligible columns, at an arbitrary embedding over p. For a gauge <=p^c, scaling by p^(-beta_p) yields gauge <=p^(c+beta_p); integral if c<=-beta_p. By ceil, beta_p<G_p-err_p+1.

Here is the full case split of exists_indexDvd_Bmu_divisible, from the native lower-level suppliers:
(i) Outside small nonsplit primes and p=N, err=G=0, beta=0, and gaugeLe_one makes the full integral column module sufficient, exponent 0. Its extra condition p≠d follows since d is small and nonsplit (native scale).

(iii) For an unserved small nonsplit prime, use the original collision bound m collisionSum(p,empty)<=L_p. The local gauge exponent is m collisionSum+L_p-G_p<=2L_p-G_p. Here err=3L_p and L_p>=1 from the native magnitudes. Thus after the integer beta correction every integral column works with index exponent 0. In particular the exact collision sum derived in worker 2776e6a5 may be used to check this case, but no unproven bypass of the served branch is needed.
(iv) For a served small nonsplit prime, the actual full marked set Lambda exists. Let T be the ordered product of its mark operators and J=jetCeil(p). HasType.prod and hasType_prod_markOperator_pow yield a submodule T^m M+p^J M of index determinant dividing p^l, l<=3L_p C_d. The two summands separately have local gauge exponent <=2L_p-G_p and <=L_p-G_p, by gaugeLe_Bmu_of_isMarked and gaugeLe_pow_jetCeil_smul. A nonarchimedean norm bounds a sum by the larger of these, so the whole submodule has the required exponent <=-beta. The submodule is chosen independently of the twist and good Psi, exactly as in the native proof. The same argument holds on its intersection/pullback to ramified columns. This supplies the local theorem from its stated Fourier, marking and chain-gauge estimates, not a new proof of those estimates.

The aggregate loss budget also has an elementary explanation. Native IsShortPrime means p=m c±1. For p<x1, p>=2, if p is short, choose such integer c>=1, c<=floor(x1); m/p<=2/c. If p is not short take c=p; 1/p<=2/c. Thus each small nonsplit err_p log p is bounded by 6fa(log b)^2/c after using log p<=log x1<=log b and, more precisely, retaining the native loss formula err_p=3 f (log b)^2 (a/p)*[m or 1]. ### B. Finite Fourier, positivity and rank: original sufficient constants

Retain native finite G, unitary rho, P normalized so its Gram matrix is the Bessel diagonal plus an error. Let R_good be the central orthogonal projection to rows. Native Petersson amplitudes and Moments give three exact inputs for the same P:
 (1) sum_i ||(P*P-I)e_i||<=delta C_d, delta=b^-4;
 (2) tr((I-R_good)PP*)<=4delta C_d;
 (3) mean_s ||P* rho(s)P||_HS² <=(1+9delta) C_d²/D_max,
where D_max=|G| besselDegree mass>0 and rowDim<=D_max. The second bound follows by the two native bad-projection trace estimates, each <=2delta C_d; commuting orthogonal projections satisfy I-R_good<=sum of the two individual bad projections. The first uses amplitudeSum_besselS_one and the native remaining row norm estimate. Energy already includes unipCovolume^-2. No extra normalization factor.

We prove the actual rank conclusion directly, including the row-dimension-zero case. Let A=PP*, T=mean_s rho(s)A rho(s)*, so by cyclicity of trace ||T||_HS²=mean_s ||P*rho(s)P||_HS². For a subspace F of rows, set phi=dim F/D_r (zero if D_r=0). Write U_s={v:R_good rho(s)P v in F}. For its orthogonal projection P_U, trace(P_U(P*P-I)) has absolute value <=sum_i ||(P*P-I)e_i|| by expanding in the column orthonormal basis and ||P_U e_i||<=1. On U_s, rho(s)P v lies in the orthogonal sum F+range(I-R_good). Extend an orthonormal basis of U_s to the column space. Positivity of squared norms yields
  dim U_s <= delta C_d + ||P_F rho(s)P||_HS² + ||(I-R_good)rho(s)P||_HS².
Average over s; the last term is constant by commutation, bounded by (2). The middle average is tr(P_F T)<=sqrt(dim F)||T||_HS. By (3) and D_r<=D_max it is <= C_d sqrt(phi(1+9delta)), also zero if D_r=0. AM-GM gives
  mean dim U_s <= C_d[(1+phi)/2 + (19/2)delta].
Choose a translate no larger than its mean. The argument is the native Fits.exists_translate_finrank_comap_le, now expressed with positive matrices instead of dyadSum tensors. Nonzero scalar normalization of P has no effect on U_s.

For the actual ramifying operator E=prod_{w|pi}(1-z_w) on columns, native kernel bound for each factor is <=C_d/(N-1); rank-nullity gives dim E^-1(U)<=dim U+#piPlaces C_d/(N-1) iteratively. #piPlaces=f<=b/1000 and N-1>=b^9; therefore (19/2)b^-4+f/(N-1)<=b^-2, using b>=1000. Coordinate equivalences and nonzero scalar normalization give the original IsSpread inequality for the actual projectedPoincare after ramification. If rows were zero, F=0 and every U_s (including after ramification) would be the entire nonzero column domain. The bound C_d(1/2+b^-2)<C_d is a contradiction. Therefore D_r>0, as required before applying the greedy lemma. Apply §3's rank-deficit potential with coefficient 5 to obtain exactly the native B, not a stronger unverified constant.

For input (3), integrate worker 025426f92fc87ece6dbe883d1b31c20a2d9b2581's human Fourier proof (full note in specialists/positivity_geometry/compact/RUN51_GLOBAL_SIEVE_AND_FINITE_FOURIER.lean). Suppose all the ordinary/contragredient fixers of nonzero frame vectors carry mass <=Y. For y,v nonzero in F_q^l, the vectors

have squared norms <=|G|Y, where e_x are the standard basis of functions on F_q^r and f_z(w)=q^-r/2 psi(-z^T w) are their Fourier basis. Their inner product equals

Cauchy-Schwarz bounds the latter character sum by q^(r/2)Y. ### C. Single determinant, comparison operator and exact height combination

Native HeightLe(c,y) is a signed bound: there is H0 nonzero, arch bound alpha for ALL complex automorphisms, local bounds e_p for ALL isomorphisms to the algebraic closure of Q_p, and alpha+sum_{p<X} e_p<=c eventually. Multiplication is direct: multiply the witnesses, add the log bounds. Finite products and powers follow. For y=1, H0 is algebraic even from its uniform arch bound alone: if H0 were transcendental, extend {H0} to a transcendence basis over Qbar. The map H0→n H0 fixing the other basis elements is a field automorphism of the rational function field, and extends to an automorphism of its algebraic closure C. Then |nH0| is unbounded, contradiction. Let K=Q(H0), a=[K:Q], N0'=Norm_K/Q H0≠0. Every embedding of K into C or Qbar_p extends to an isomorphism of the relevant algebraic closures of transcendence-basis fields (as in native exists_ringEquiv_extend); hence the product of the a arch conjugates gives log|N0'|/a<=alpha and at p log|N0'|_p/a<=e_p. The rational product formula, summed over all sufficiently large finite prime sets containing the support of N0', gives 0<=c. Signed e_p are not absolute heights. No positivity of each e_p is assumed.

Now take one full basis x_i of rows, where n original independent short rows y_i are completed by k_aux=D_r-n crude rows. Let G_0 and G_theta denote Gram matrices of their lifts. G_0 is positive definite, H0=det G_0≠0. The native normDet ratio gives det G_theta/det G_0=z_theta/z_1. Its arch witness bound follows by Hadamard or orthogonal completion: log bound for old family V plus sum of the k_aux crude log squared norms. The native crude_rows gives a common nonzero gamma_vartheta for auxiliary forms, whose ratio gamma(theta_i)/gamma(1) controls integrality, and HeightLe for |gamma(theta_i)/gamma(1)|^-2. At p and embedding j, choose u_j from IndexLe, and extend block diagonally by gamma_rel I_(k_aux), with gamma_rel=gamma(theta_i)/gamma(1). For the resulting matrix m_j,
  |j(det m_j)|>=exp(-e_p)|j(gamma_rel)|^k_aux.
The matrix for j∘conj can differ! Pair each of its lifted combinations with each of the j-combinations; the native norm_inner_le_one makes all entries of
  conjugate(m_(j∘conj)) G_theta (m_j)^T
integral under j (convention: rows are coefficients, inner product conjugate-linear in first slot). Its determinant is thus <=1 in p-adic norm. Multiplicativity of determinant gives
  |j(det G_theta)| <= exp(2e_p)|j(gamma_rel conj gamma_rel)|^(-k_aux).
Since det G_theta = H0*(z_theta/z_1), equivalently H0*(z_theta/z_1)*|gamma_rel|^(2k_aux) satisfies local bounds 2e_p. Multiply HeightLe for |gamma_rel|^-2 to the power k_aux, yielding
  HeightLe(alpha+2delta+k_aux g, z_theta/z_1)
where g is the native gamma-cost bound. This proves one_determinant directly, including the two embedding matrices. Native crude_rows and magnitude guards bound the total auxiliary arch and gamma costs by D_r b for k_aux<=4D_r/b². Therefore HeightLe(V+2delta+D_r b, z_theta/z_1). No assumption that j commutes with complex conjugation.

For each lift j, let u_vartheta=goodLift(vartheta,j), gl_vartheta the native comparison forms, and h_j=smallHookNumber. Write the actual adjoint comparison identity u_vartheta* gl_vartheta = gamma_vartheta A, with A surjective independent of vartheta, gamma nonzero, |gamma_vartheta/gamma_1|²=heckeValueProd(j,vartheta)/heckeValueProd(j,1). The compressed operator K_vartheta=h_j pr_(im u) gl gl*|_(im u) has determinant k_vartheta. Since u is injective, identify its domain with im u using u, or choose orthonormal bases on both spaces. Direct multiplication gives
  det(u* K_vartheta u)=det(u*u) det K_vartheta
                    = h_j^D_r det[(gl*u)* (gl*u)].
Taking adjoints in the comparison identity gives gl*u=conj(gamma) A*, and surjectivity of A makes A* injective. Therefore
  lambda_vartheta k_vartheta = h_j^D_r |gamma_vartheta|^(2D_r) det(AA*),
with lambda_vartheta=normDet(u)^2, and both sides nonzero. In particular
  lambda_1/lambda_vartheta = (k_vartheta/k_1) (heckeValueProd(j,vartheta)/heckeValueProd(j,1))^(-D_r).

### A: finite cuts and the exact denominator of unfolded periods

The actual IntegralAsCosetSum.exists_cut_coverMultiplicity_dvd has levels J<=A, an ambient subgroup B>=A, a subgroup S acting on the left on G_f/J, a compact D0 contained in B, and a set W of relevant points all movable into D0 by S. For each x in D0 assume some integer n has (x u x^-1)^(q^n) in A for every u in J; q is the actual twist levelNorm (positive). The required cut is finite, (S intersect A)-stable, inside B/J, with cover multiplicity positive and dividing a power of q at every relevant coset.

Proof: G_f/J is discrete since J is open. Thus D0 has finite image. Choose exactly one coset xJ from that image for each S-orbit it meets, and saturate this finite family under S intersect A to obtain T. This is finite because its image is contained in that of compact A D0, is invariant by construction, and stays inside B/J. Each relevant point meets one of the selected orbits. For such an orbit write t=xJ, S_A=S intersect A, H=Stab_S(t)=S intersect xJx^-1. For s in S, s^-1 t is in T iff s^-1 t lies in S_A t, since T contains no representative from a different orbit in this orbit. Equivalently s belongs to H S_A. Consequently the number of distinct right-S_A cosets sS_A for which t belongs to sT is exactly [H:H intersect S_A]. This is the actual coverMultiplicity; it is invariant under replacing t by a translate in its S-orbit, by left multiplication of the covering cosets. The index is finite: H is contained in compact xJx^-1, and intersecting with the open subgroup A gives a finite number of cosets in H (use the finite coset image of compact xJx^-1 in G_f/A). The stabilizer of each coset in the finite permutation action of H on H/(H intersect S_A) contains the corresponding conjugate of H intersect S_A. Let N_core be the intersection of these stabilizers (normal core). For all h in H, h^(q^n) belongs to H intersect S_A by the stated hypothesis, and the same is true of every H-conjugate of h. Thus h^(q^n) lies in N_core. The finite quotient H/N_core has exponent dividing q^n, so by Cauchy's theorem all prime divisors of its order divide q. Its order, and hence [H:H intersect S_A], divides a power of q. If q=1 the index is 1. This proves the actual cut lemma, including nonzero multiplicities on W and T. No assumption that the stabilizer is normal in H.

The native integral equality uses the same cut. Normalize Haar eta on S so eta(S_A)=1. Put psi(t)=1_T(t)/mult(t), zero when not in T. For each relevant t, the set {s:s^-1 t in T} is a disjoint union of mult(t) cosets of S_A by the previous count (or the definition for an arbitrary finite stable T with nonzero multiplicity). Hence
  integral_S psi(s^-1 t) d eta(s)=1.
At points where f vanishes the cutoff has no effect on the integral, even if their multiplicity is zero. This is a genuine partition of unity, not a formal division by the number of all orbits.

In Grading.exists_integral_eq_sum_over_cut retain the actual hypotheses: f=P y integrable on the rational Bessel quotient, P generic and y automorphic, so the product is invariant under the rational Bessel group; the finite level J; stable finite T; and the factorization of the Bessel orbit period at real/finite point t=(t_inf,t_f) as stdLevelIndex(J)*a(t_inf)*b(t_f). The proper-action cutoff beta and alpha(t_inf)=integral beta(t_inf,s)d eta(s) in the source provide the disintegration of the rational Bessel quotient. By quotient integration in stages (with the preceding finite partition of unity), integrate the Bessel orbit period against alpha(t_inf) psi(t_f) and the real/finite measures. Factorization and Fubini give the product of the real integral kappa=integral alpha a and the finite integral of psi b times stdLevelIndex(J). Each finite coset tJ has volume mu(J); standard finite-level normalization is stdLevelIndex(J)*mu(J)=1 (the native proof uses mu(K_f)=1 and J<=K_f). Thus exactly
  integral_(ratBessel quotient) f = kappa sum_(t in T) b(t)/mult(t).
For use at the native Seesaw caller keep its exact Haar choices and integrability, not an invented absolute volume formula. The scalar kappa depends on A and a and the chosen measure/cutoff, not on twist or J or the selected finite b function. The native proper-action cutoff and quotient integration theorem are retained as prerequisites; the derivation above is their explicit consumer, not a proof that all adelic quotients admit a cutoff without hypotheses.

Now read SeesawTheorem.exists_stage_cut and exists_isAdmissibleComb_unfolding. At stage k, the actual cut is stable under the stage Bessel subgroup intersect A, and its mult divides a power of levelNorm(ϑ). At k=0 the ambient level is chosenLevel; at k>0 it is the maximal compact with local integrality away from twist primes. Given the native stage-cut support, one stage of the unfolding replaces the linear-value function S(y) by
  S_k(y)=sum_(t in T_k) mult(t)^-1 S(update(y,k,t)).
Each coefficient is integral at every embedding away from primes dividing levelNorm(ϑ), because the positive integer mult has only these prime divisors. There is no ramified-prime denominator from the integer N used to build the columns in this step. If the twist is trivial, every mult is 1, and the native bound |T_k|<=cutBound gives l1 size amplification <=cutBound. Stability under stage level action follows by permuting t and using mult invariance; previous stage actions commute with this update. The native arrow adjunction and stage period unfolding (isArrow_datumY and integral_besselGroup_unfold) identify the next period with this sum times the scalar independent of twist. Induct from the base period given by integral_poincare: at depth k, the coefficient vector has total size <=cutBound^k at trivial twist, and integrality away from levelNorm for every twist; the scalar is common to all twists. This reconstructs the finite-sum/denominator induction in exists_isAdmissibleComb_unfolding. The final reduced vacuum and last-stage maps in seesaw combine this datum using the native invariant-combination closure and equality of inner products against every test form. The common normalization is normUnit(a*C/cutBound^(2m)), as in the source; do not silently replace normUnit by an arbitrary nonzero scalar, since the zero case of its definition is handled in the native proof. The stage support and near-line geometry of exists_stage_cut, construction of reduced vacuum and final-stage maps, and the native adjunction/unfolding identities remain deep A prerequisites. No claim that the entire seesaw is now independently proved.

### B: Schur comparison and cut-to-full energy bookkeeping

Actual SchurTest uses class cutoffs beta_c^0 and frame volume Delta(y_v)>0, exponent k+n with n=rho+1. On the support of the cut Bessel kernel both y and gamma^-1 y are in the required cut. Let
  w(y)=prod_v (Delta(y_v)/Delta((gamma^-1 y)_v))^(k+n)>0.
The integrand of amplitudeAbs is bounded by the geometric mean of the two nonnegative functions
  F(y)=beta_c^0(y) beta_c'^0(gamma^-1 y) ||W(y)||² w(y),
  G(y)=beta_c^0(y) beta_c'^0(gamma^-1 y) ||W(gamma^-1 y)||² /w(y).
Here ||W||² denotes the product of the local squared norms; the kernel norm estimate is Cauchy-Schwarz in each local coefficient space and the cutoff identity on its support, exactly as in amplitudeAbs_sq_le. The ratio of volumes cancels BEFORE integration. By integral Cauchy-Schwarz, amplitudeAbs²<=(integral F)(integral G). The first is schurIntegral(c,c',gamma). By the Haar-invariant change of variable y=gamma z the second is schurIntegral(c',c,gamma^-1), since w(gamma z)^-1 is the reversed volume ratio. This proves the actual Schur comparison.

The source's averaging inequality uses the cutoff disintegration along the unipotent subgroup U0, with orbit-mass total at most nu * #BesselCoord (native lintegral_sq_cut_le and lintegral_classCutoff_mul_prod). For a nonnegative F invariant under the rational subgroup, its integral with this cutoff and Bessel squared norm is unchanged by right translation of the U0 integration variable by s(x) in U0. Use this invariance for each x in the auxiliary measurable set B0 of measure >=1; integrate and apply Tonelli. The assumed uniform average integral_B0 F(u s(x)y) <= C for all u and boxed y then bounds the original integral by C times the cutoff mass. Thus exactly the native lintegral_boxCutoff_le_of_average bound C nu #BesselCoord. The actual disintegration/cutoff mass and rational invariance must remain; arbitrary translation of the original global integral alone would not justify this.

In CrudeEnergy.energy_le_of_level_pairs, the actual amplitudes split by link Lambda. For each (c,s,c'), expand the squared norm of the sum by triangle inequality, retain the diagonal Lambda=Lambda' in R exactly and charge every other ordered pair to the pair-weight estimate. The native lemma establishes that each such remainder term is bounded by
  (C/τ³)*nu²*(pairWeight(j,c,c',γ,γ')+pairWeight(j,c,c',γ',γ))
after expanding each link bound into its rational terms. Summing over ordered pairs uses the native injectivity of the index map (no double use of link labels) and sum_pairWeight_le; its scale guards give <= C nu²/[b^30 4^(rho+1-j)]. Divide by nu² to recover cut energy <= w+C/[b^30 4^(rho+1-j)]. This is the exact algebraic sum consumer. The quantitative pointwise pair-weight bound itself (pairInvariant_mul_le, exists_link_split_le_classGauge, classSize and sum_pairWeight_le) remains a geometric supplier; we have not proved it by Schur Cauchy-Schwarz alone.

For completeness the cut-to-full arithmetic requires no more of that geometry. If each character-compatible term has level below n0, use amplitudeSum_eq_zero_of_not_charsAgree for level-free terms. Partition by the highest remaining level, and use ||a+b||²<=2||a||²+2||b||². Induction gives the native cut energy bound C/[b^30 4^(rho+1-n0)]: base n0=0 is zero; for n0=j+1 the bound for the lower levels and level j alone is at most C/[b^30 4^(rho+1-j)] each, multiplied by 2 and added, giving exactly the next factor of four. The stability of the level partition and the character vanishing are the actual native hypotheses. At the last level n0=rho+1, the degenerate/discriminant-zero cut part has energy <=C/b^30. The nonzero-discriminant part has cut energy <=w+C/b^30. Put delta=b^-4. For any amplitudes A,B,
  E(A+B)<=(1+delta) E(A)+(1+delta^-1)E(B)
by weighted Cauchy-Schwarz, using the actual normalization nu^-2. Apply it to these two cut parts and then to full=cut+(full-cut). Native energy_sub_cut_le <=b^-30 D1<=b^-30 C. This yields
  E(full)<=(1+delta)^2 w
       + C b^-30[(1+delta)^2+(1+delta)(1+delta^-1)+(1+delta^-1)].
For b>=1000 the bracket is 3+3delta+delta²+2delta^-1 (direct expansion actually 4+3delta+delta²+2delta^-1; use the latter, exact). It is <=10 b^4, so the entire remainder is <=b^-12 C. This is the original energy_le_of_linkEnergy_le with its exact constants, not a change of the required w. Retain C's conditions involving D1, tau^20 D1 when a normal-form-zero term occurs, and tau^12 mainEnergy/X^k when links are omitted. For energy_fixingSet_le the difficult remaining step is the coherent link-energy w bound from the actual local Bessel kernel and global sieve, as well as the quantitative pointwise pair-weight supplier just isolated. We did not prove those by an assumed random distribution of terms.

### C: one valuation-ring combination of eigenoperators, then one determinant

The actual norm_gram_det_le_of_operators (New/Selmer/DeterminantBound.lean) assumes A spans Z of dimension d, x_b in Z, a linear map j:V→W, linear functionals eta_a (a,b=1,...,d), and a scalar m in the local field. At each nonzero p in A there is a linear G_p, diagonal relative to j on every v in A: G_p v=l_p(v)j(v). Every scalar l with G_p p=l j(p) satisfies |iota(l)m|>=exp(-gamma). Also for every w in Z with j(w)=G_p(x_b), the pairing bound |m iota(eta_a(w))|<=1 holds for all a,b. These are the original hypotheses; no common eigenoperator is assumed, no injectivity of j is assumed.

Choose a basis omega_1,...,omega_d from A (if d=0 determinant 1 proves the result). For each basis element choose its G_i and write G_i omega_k=l_ik j(omega_k). The diagonal condition gives |m iota(l_kk)|>=e^-gamma. Write x_b=sum_k P_bk omega_k. For each i,b put w_ib=sum_k P_bk l_ik omega_k in Z, so j(w_ib)=G_i x_b by linearity. The native pairing assumption gives |m iota(eta_a(w_ib))|<=1.

There exists ONE vector d_i in the valuation ring of the algebraic closure of Q_l such that simultaneously for every k,
  |a_k|=|sum_i d_i m iota(l_ik)|=max_i |m iota(l_ik)|.
Proof: each column has nonzero maximum by its diagonal bound. Divide its coefficients by a maximizing coefficient; their reductions to the residue field Fbar_l are not all zero. The bad choices of the reduced vector (d_i) form a finite union of proper linear hyperplanes. An infinite field is not covered by finitely many proper linear hyperplanes (use the nonzero product polynomial and the elementary infinite-field polynomial lemma). Choose a vector outside them and lift its coordinates to the valuation ring. The normalized sum is a unit for every column, yielding the equality. This is an elementary proof of the actual exists_val_coeff_le_val_eval use; it requires an infinite residue field, available here, not a claim for arbitrary fixed finite residue fields.

Now the matrix
  M_ab = sum_i d_i m iota(eta_a(w_ib))
has entries of norm<=1. By substituting w_ib it factors exactly as
  M = [iota(eta_a(omega_k))] diag(a_k) [iota(P_bk)]^T.
Therefore
  1>=|det M|=|iota(det[eta_a(x_b)])| prod_k |a_k|
            >=|iota(det[eta_a(x_b)])| exp(-d gamma).
This proves the actual determinant bound exp(d gamma), avoiding separate losses for each pair a,b. If the determinant is zero the inequality is automatic. The same combination works for every matrix entry; using different d vectors for rows would invalidate the factorization. The local field in the native theorem is as given, and absolute values are those of iota; the coefficient m is not replaced by a positive real scalar.

The actual denominatorLe_rowBudget uses this lemma with j=toSmoothForm, eta_a from the integral comparison family Xi, x_b the projected tested Xi'_b, and m the chosenGroupOrder^3 normalization. The required G_p is the actual Hecke diagonal operator E_s composed with tupleProj O c, where c is the chosen label of p. The native body proves that on each vector in the spanning set of labelled, lifted eigenspaces this operator is scalar relative to j: other labels are killed by orthogonal idempotents, same labels are diagonal by the base-level eigensystem. The lower bound for its eigenvalue is supplied by the tied-prime Ribet/prime-triangle argument via exists_diagOp_mem_ellIntegralForms, with the native SelmerLengthLe budget for that SAME twist, and the upper pairing bound by class-automorphism divisibility and the integral test projection. We keep these as explicit unmet inputs rather than assuming the generic normalizer, triangle, local duality or test-orthogonality argument. This establishes the linear algebra of the actual consumer and isolates exactly what arithmetic must supply. DenominatorLe uses all integral embeddings for the first family and integrality plus testedness for the second at the selected iota; no exchange of these quantifiers is permitted.
