## 6f. C: the uniform odd-Sha arithmetic chain — Voronoi, annihilator, detecting points

Integrate literature RUN50 (780bada88d76a9314f517f2394e0a3d66e2776b9) into the actual C consumer, with the following human derivation of the connected ComponentBound steps. All symbols here are local to this subsection: N is the cyclotomic conductor, e>0 the Tate weight, l a prime, O the native p-adic integer DVR with finite quotients, nu the torsion exponent, and psi a character modulo N such that cyc^e psi is odd. Do not identify N with the fixed analytic modulus 18480. Write X=X_e for the single positive Stickelberger integer, chosen before l, O, psi and nu. No new RH or GRH premise.

**Uniform integer.** Put n=e+1, D=(e+3)!(e+2)N, p(t)=(e+3)!N^(e+2) B_(e+2)(t/N), b(t)=p'(t). The Bernoulli recurrence makes (j+1)! B_j integral, so p is an integer polynomial, and p(t+N)-p(t)=D t^n. For f=Nm, F=sum_(0<=t<f) floor(ct/f)t^e psi(t), S=sum psi(t)t^n and B=sum_(0<=t<N) psi(t)b(t), telescoping and first-order Taylor give D S=f B mod f². Reindex ct by its residue modulo f, for c=1 mod N coprime to m, to obtain (c^n-1)S=n c^e f F mod f². Cancellation in the characteristic-zero domain gives (c^n-1)B=D n c^e F mod f. This cancellation is performed BEFORE passing to a finite quotient.

On the unit group modulo N use the integer matrix T_(u,v)=g(v/u), g(t)=2b(rep(t))+(-1)^e [t=1]+[t=-1]. Its eigenvalue on psi is 2B+(-1)^e+psi(-1). The standard Bernoulli special value gives B=-Dn L(-e,psi); matching parity has B=0 and eigenvalue 2(-1)^e, opposite parity has B≠0 by the primitive functional equation and absolute convergence at 1+e, with every removed Euler factor nonzero. For N=1 use B_n(0)=B_n(1) for n>=2. The complex character basis proves det T≠0. Over ANY allowed characteristic-zero domain the adjugate applied to the character vector and its coordinate at 1 proves 2B divides det T for opposite parity, without dividing by the group order. Take X=|det T| product_(c=1+N,1+2N)(c^e-1)(c^(e+1)-1). These two c are coprime, so every prime has one eligible c with the product dividing X. For any c=1 mod N, X=2B u for some u in O, and the preceding cancellation gives (c^(e+1)-1)X=2w F mod m with w=u D n c^e independent of m. The peer's Leibniz bound, using |B_n(z)|<=4n! on [0,1], gives X<=2^((N+e+3)^6). This supplies the native IsStickelbergerBound, not a claim based on checking a finite set of primes.

**1. Exact removal of nonunits, then the odd-pair identity.** For k>=0 set M_k=N l^k and F_k=sum_(0<=t<M_k) floor(a t/M_k)t^e psi(t), for a=1 mod N coprime to l. Write S_k for the native sum of floorDiff_a(t/M_k)t^e psi(t) over units modulo M_k. For k>=1 the nonunits divisible by a prime of N have weight zero. The remaining nonunits are exactly accounted for by t=l u, with floor(a l u/M_k)=floor(a u/M_(k-1)). Consequently the sum of the floor terms over units is EXACTLY F_k-l^e psi(l)F_(k-1), including l|N when psi(l)=0.

The actual floorDiff_a(x)=floor(a {x})-floor(a {-x}). Reindex its second sum by t→-t modulo M_k. The weight becomes its negative MODULO l^k by odd parity, not as an exact integer polynomial evaluation. Thus
  S_k = 2(F_k-l^e psi(l)F_(k-1)) mod l^k.
The uniform Voronoi relation at k and k-1 can be subtracted after multiplication by l^e psi(l), since e>=1. It yields
  (a^(e+1)-1)X(1-l^e psi(l)) = w S_k mod l^k.
The parenthetic factor is a unit in the quotient O/l^k. Therefore S_k divides (a^(e+1)-1)X there, exactly sum_floorDiff_dvd. For k=0 the quotient is the zero ring. This proof does not invert 2 or phi(N), and is valid at l=2.

**2. From the Jacobi family and Artin reciprocity to a uniform annihilator.** The native Jacobi family J(x) (written multiplicatively) is cyclotomically equivariant, a unit off its selected prime p=1 mod M_nu, and has valuation floorDiff_a(x) at the selected place. Its further distribution and principal-unit properties are retained for the Kolyvagin step. The elementary distribution law used in its construction follows because the representatives of x+j/r are (j+u)/r after permutation, with u={r x}. For coprime a,r, summing floor(a(j+u)/r) gives floor(a u)+(a-1)(r-1)/2 by permuting a j modulo r. Apply this for x and -x; the common constant cancels, giving sum_j floorDiff_a(x+j/r)=floorDiff_a(r x). The actual Gauss-sum construction is now expanded in peer 4c8f4b0b (RUN53): set G_k=sum_(a=1)^(p−1) omega(a)^(-k) zeta^a, pi=zeta−1. In its binomial expansion the coefficient at degree k is −1/k! mod p and lower coefficients vanish mod p, hence ord_p G_k=k/(p−1) by the unique least valuation. Then J(x)=G(t)^c G(−ct)/(G(ct)G(−t)^c), with x=k/(p−1) in the actual character parametrization. Cyclotomic change of variable cancels character phases; Gauss products give off-p units and conjugate modulus one; the floor distribution above at every conjugate place and Kronecker give the torsion distribution. At any q|p−1 a q-th root reduces to 1, so the Gauss quotient is a principal unit at every place over q. This supplies the same family and all six quantified fields as a human derivation; audit its use in the derivative transition, not just the scalar valuation.

For z in the native component (locally constant cocycle for (cyc^e psi)^(-1), zero at complex conjugation c and on the prescribed inertia and decomposition groups), suppose 4X² z≠0. Choose a as supplied by IsStickelbergerBound with (a^e-1)(a^(e+1)-1)|X. The cocycle 2X² z cannot vanish identically on the cyclotomic subgroup for N l^nu: if it did, it would factor through the abelian cyclotomic quotient, and commutation with c, using the odd character and its zero value at c, would give 4X² z=0. Chebotarev therefore supplies a prime p=1 mod N l^nu and a Frobenius sigma with 2X² z(sigma)≠0.

Apply the actual artin_law_component to y=J(1/M_nu), the same z, sigma and a. The integer valuation coefficients are floorDiff_a(t/M_nu) by equivariance; all off-l,p unit conditions hold with r=1 (additive zero in the native units notation). That theorem gives S_nu * 2(a^e-1) z(sigma)=0 in O/l^nu. Step 1 allows multiplication by the witness of divisibility to give 2(a^e-1)(a^(e+1)-1)X z(sigma)=0. Since the product divides X, this gives 2X² z(sigma)=0, a contradiction. Hence
  4X² z=0
for ALL primes and torsion exponents. This is the actual nsmul_component_eq_zero argument, using the retained Artin principal-idele identity and native Jacobi supplier, not a statement that all higher cohomology is finite simply by class field theory. No division by a nonunit.

**3. Large-prime detection: reconstruct the descending Kolyvagin argument.** Assume l>N+e+3 and let m=v_l(X)+1. Choose a≡1 mod N, primitive modulo l, by CRT; 2, phi(Nl), a^e-1 and a^(e+1)-1 are units modulo l, since 0<e<e+1<l-1. Choose an inertia element gamma with (cyc^(e+1) psi)(gamma)=a^(e+1), by the native inertia supplier. Let Y(T) be the finite module of cyc^(e+1) psi-cocycles in O/l^m vanishing on inertia over Q(mu_N) away from l and the primes of T, and Y=Y(empty). Let Z consist of nonzero component cocycles killed by l. Use the source's finite unramified-cocycle theorem (finite coefficient module and fixed ramification set) for finiteness; no estimate on the number of these cocycles is needed.

Suppose no m evaluations detect the component. For a set T of <=m primes congruent to 1 mod Nl, choose their Frobenii (pad the list to m). There is a nonzero component cocycle zero at these Frobenii, and multiplying by a maximal power of l retaining a nonzero result gives w in Z with the same zeros. At a split prime off l, inertia is killed by the native component conditions, the decomposition action on the l-torsion coefficients is trivial, and the decomposition quotient is topologically generated by Frobenius. Thus w kills every decomposition group at each prime of T (conjugation preserves the zeros).

Write J(T) for: the primes of T split modulo Nl, and every l-killed member of Y(T) belongs to Y. This holds for empty T. If J(T) holds and a split prime q detects w, then q is not in T and J(T∪{q}) holds. Indeed for an l-killed cocycle k in Y(T∪{q}), reciprocity_mod_ell applies at q with w, because at every prime in T the decomposition values of w vanish and 2(a^e-1) is a unit. It kills the new inertia at q; J(T) finishes the extension. This uses the actual reciprocity theorem, not a claim that adjoining arbitrary primes leaves the module unchanged.

For each pair (y,w) in the finite Y×Z with y nonzero on the cyclotomic subgroup for a N l^m, choose a prime q=1 mod a N l^m and a Frobenius detecting both y and w. Such a choice is possible by Chebotarev: two proper subgroup intersections cannot cover the cyclotomic subgroup; w is nonzero there by oddness and l odd. Collect these primes in one finite set Q. Choose initial w for empty T, and a prime p=1 mod N l^m product_(q in Q) q detecting it, hence J({p}). The source exists_kolyvagin_cocycles gives a single family kappa_S for S⊂Q with kappa_S(gamma)=0, ramification restricted to {p}∪S, the bottom-class implication, and the exact relation kappa_S(Frob_q)=kappa_(S∪{q})(tau_q) for some inertia tau_q whenever q∉S. This is the deep Gauss-sum derivative supplier, retained as an explicit input.

**Local derivative supplier, independently audited (integration RUN56, 26 November).** The previous explicit input is now expanded in [integration/2026-11-26_COMPACT_KOLYVAGIN_LOCAL_TRANSITION.lean](integration/2026-11-26_COMPACT_KOLYVAGIN_LOCAL_TRANSITION.lean), following literature RUN52 and integration 40f4331a. Use additive notation for multiplicative algebraic units, m=l^n, M0=N*m, M=M0 prod I, and m*y(x)=J(x), with mu identifying Z/m with mth roots. The derivative is mu(F_g(x))=gamma*y(x)-g gamma g^-1*y(x); kappa_I=Lambda_I(0,F_g). Jacobi distribution and unit reindexing remove all offsets exactly, and give the twisted cocycle law. At inertia off l,p,I fixing mu_N, the Mth roots are fixed, the difference of y roots is principal-unit and m-torsion, hence zero; the same argument applied to gamma*y proves the required ramification. At B over p choose m*r0=p and tame inertia tau with tau*r0-r0=mu(1). The unit y(x)-floorDiff(c,x)*r0 gives F_tau(x)=a_gamma*floorDiff(c,x)-floorDiff(c,a_gamma*x). Linearity now gives a direct induction on the chain: if ν^j times the current vector has Weyl gauge <=1, apply the raising map for the ν-scaled datum to this vector. ## 6h. Integrated A/C supplier batch and correction of the finite pencil argument (integration, 26 November)

Source pin and attribution unchanged. Mission blob 23f64d503b7d64bb13b9667156b499021038d742. Read the current editor manuscript and peer commits b67157a1, fa58872b, bf254a7b, 2999f952, 898feb42, d93adfe1, a144d8de, 3180f6da, 6653e0ac, c78302e2, ef865994, c7cde9ac and 15c60307. The editor already incorporates the signed repayment, Fourier Sha bridge and frequency suppliers in §6g; do not duplicate them.

### C: corrected generic-slope lemma and uniform finite pencil

The math peer d93adfe1 (math/2026-11-26_COMPACT_PENCIL_DESCENT.md, blob 8b26f5cbd5a4447ce0d57741c7cde140878338f3) gives the correct overall divisor descent, but its §3.3 erroneously says that Φ1 factors through Φ0 on Z = sum_s (Y ∩ ker(sΦ0−Φ1)). This is false even over F5: Φ0(x,y)=x+y, Φ1(x,y)=x+2y. The vectors (1,0),(0,1) lie in the slope-1 and slope-2 kernels respectively, so Z=F5², but Φ0(1,4)=0 and Φ1(1,4)=4. No such factorization may be assumed. The actual native proof uses projective lifts instead.

Here is the corrected human argument for the finite-length Selmer consumer. Write O for the DVR, A=O/(a), and Φ0,Φ1:Y→A^h for the paired tame-inertia and Frobenius maps. Put Z=sum_s ker(sΦ0−Φ1) inside Y. By linearity Φ1(Z)⊆Φ0(Z) (only inclusion of images, not factorization). Let P⊂O^h be the inverse image of Φ0(Z) under reduction. P is free of rank at most h. The reduction q:P→Φ0(Z) is onto, and so is Φ0:Z→Φ0(Z). By projectivity choose σ:P→Z with Φ0σ=q. Lift Φ1σ:P→Φ0(Z) through q to ψ:P→P. For any s whose residue avoids the roots of the reduced monic characteristic polynomial of ψ, sI−ψ is invertible over O. Then (sΦ0−Φ1)σ=q(sI−ψ) is onto Φ0(Z); the opposite image inclusion follows from Φ1(Z)⊆Φ0(Z). Consequently by additivity of finite lengths,
  length ker((sΦ0−Φ1)|Z) = length Z − length Φ0(Z)
    = length(Z ∩ ker Φ0)
    ≤ length(Y ∩ ker Φ0 ∩ Φ1⁻¹(Φ0(Y))).
The slope kernel on Y equals that on Z by definition. The bad residue list has at most h entries. This proves the exact inequality of tilting at the actual finite consumer, without assuming that Φ0 is injective or that Φ1 descends to its image. General infinite-length version remains the native theorem; no infinite subtraction is used here.

For the subsequent descent distinguish OLD ties and the newly RELAXED prime. For fixed existing J and x, write X(T;k) for the Selmer module at S∪T∪conj(T) with relations L ∪ tieRelations(J,x,i) ∪ {c(γ_i)=0}, coefficient O/(k). The native unseen criterion compares X(J;k) to the preimage of evalConjPair(γ_P)(X(J∪{P};k)) under evalConjPair(φ_P). The right side uses OLD J ties only, not a newly tied module. At the new prime write Y=X(J∪{P};k), Φ0=evalConjPair(γ_P), Φ1=evalConjPair(φ_P). Then X(J;k)=Y∩ker Φ0: the forward inclusion is unramifiedness, the reverse is the actual tame detection at both conjugate places, as in the source's I inclusion. Define r_k=length X(J;k)−length(X(J;k)∩Φ1⁻¹(Φ0(Y))). ### C: the mirror cardinal estimate follows by one bilinear polarization

Actual source: New/Selmer/LinePointPowers.lean, blob 654567bac00341a29102ee3b83266bda10ab22a9, card_le_torsion_of_isotropic (19–53). Write M=(O/I)^n. Keep the exact equivariance hW, full O-linear matrix span of rho(G), and separation of w by x_w on U. The x_w are additive Q/Z-valued functionals, not O-linear maps into Q/Z.

Fix w, abbreviate x=x_w. On U, x and y are additive in the group argument. Conjugation by k sends y(d) to rho(k)y(d), and x(d)(m) to x(d)(chi(k)rho(k)^(-1)m). where A=x(b)(t y(d)), B=x(d)(t y(b)), C=x(cb)(t y(cd)), D=x(cd)(t y(cb)).
All four are additive functions of t. Replace b by gamma b gamma^(-1), still in H, and replace t by st. The scalar laws above give
  A(t)+B(s²t)=C(s²t)+D(t).
Subtract BEFORE making any cardinal estimate:
  (B−C)((s²−1)t)=0.
Since s²−1=(s−1)(s+1) is a unit, B=C and hence A=D, over any such O, without dividing in Q/Z.

Choose tau=[g0,gamma]∈H for the unit coordinate of y(g0); y(tau)=(1−s)y(g0) has the same unit-coordinate property. Set d=k tau k^(-1). The isolated cross identity A=D gives the native intermediate C:
  x(b)(t rho(k)y(tau)) = x(c tau)(chi(c k)t rho(c k)^(-1)y(cb)).
Set b=c tau. Compare k with kg at the actual scalar pair rho(g)=alpha I_n, rho(cg)^(-1)=alpha' I_n. The left changes by substituting alpha t; the right by chi(cg)alpha' t. Their difference proves x(c tau) annihilates every (alpha−chi(cg)alpha')t rho(c k)^(-1)y(tau), since chi(c k) is a unit (a group character). The O-span of these vectors before the scalar difference is ALL M: the matrices rho(c k)^(-1) range over rho(G), and the full matrix span together with one unit entry in y(tau) produces each coordinate vector using a single-entry matrix. Thus x(c tau) descends to M/delta M, delta=alpha−chi(cg)alpha'.

If x_w(c tau)=x_v(c tau), the cross identity and the same matrix-span argument show equality of x_w(b),x_v(b) for every b∈H. For a∈U, the commutator [a,gamma]=a(gamma a^(-1)gamma^(-1)) lies in H; covariance makes its functional equal to x(a)((1−s^(-1))·). The factor 1−s^(-1) is a unit, so the functionals agree on all U. The native separation hypothesis gives w=v. Consequently the map w↦x_w(c tau) is an injection into Hom(M/delta M,Q/Z). Finite Pontryagin duality and the kernel-image cardinal identity for multiplication by delta give
  |W| ≤ |M/delta M| = |ker(delta:M→M)|.
This proves the exact native mirror lemma, conditional only on its stated hypotheses, not on a further mirror-rank bound. The proof uses the group action convention [g,h]=ghg^(-1)h^(-1); no assumption that y is globally additive.

For any additive scalar character from the m-torsion coefficient module into Z/m, the resulting restricted character belongs to a fixed finite unramified character group of U; take the common kernel of that group, then its finite-index normal core V in G. At y0, Smith normal form shows that the length of the simultaneous kernel of its rows acting on (O/(m))^d is v(det A(y0)): the elementary divisors all divide m, so the kernel lengths are exactly their valuations. ```lean
import New.Selmer.LinePointPowers
namespace CompactEditorMirror
theorem isolate_cross {R A : Type*} [CommRing R] [AddCommGroup A]
    (a b c d : R →+ A) (s : R) (hs : IsUnit (s*s-1))
    (h1 : ∀ t, a t + b t = c t + d t)
    (h2 : ∀ t, a t + b (s*s*t) = c (s*s*t) + d t) : b = c := by
  have diff (t : R) : b ((s*s-1)*t) = c ((s*s-1)*t) := by
    rw [sub_mul, one_mul, map_sub, map_sub]
    have hh : b (s*s*t) + c t = c (s*s*t) + b t := by
      apply add_left_cancel (a := a t)
      calc
        a t + (b (s*s*t) + c t) = (a t + b (s*s*t)) + c t := by abel
        _ = (c (s*s*t) + d t) + c t := by rw [h2 t]
        _ = c (s*s*t) + (c t + d t) := by abel
        _ = c (s*s*t) + (a t + b t) := by rw [← h1 t]
        _ = a t + (c (s*s*t) + b t) := by abel
    exact (sub_eq_sub_iff_add_eq_add).2 hh
  ext t
  obtain ⟨u, hu⟩ := hs
  simpa [← hu, ← mul_assoc] using diff ((↑u⁻¹ : R)*t)
#print axioms isolate_cross
#check card_le_torsion_of_isotropic
#check exists_sharp_fitting_poly
#check exists_corepresenting_coords
#print axioms card_le_torsion_of_isotropic
#print axioms exists_sharp_fitting_poly
end CompactEditorMirror
```

**Hodge frame before continuity.** [The native Hodge-frame proof](integration/2026-11-26_COMPACT_HODGE_FRAME_WITHOUT_CONTINUITY.md) removes the previously retained continuity circle. The column P a is divisible by t, so dividing that column by t and increasing k_nu by 1 gives another candidate with smaller determinant order. Contradiction. Thus P is invertible. ```lean
import New.Filling.AmplitudeSums
import Mathlib.Tactic

namespace RHProofEditorCutBudget

theorem cubic_slack (n k r : ℝ) (hn : 0 ≤ n) (hk : 1 ≤ k)
    (hrlo : k + 1 ≤ r) (hrhi : r ≤ 2 * k)
    (hguard : 24 * n^3 + 4 ≤ 3 * k) :
    k^3 + 12 * n^3 * r + 2 * k + 2 ≤ r^3 := by
  have hkn : 0 ≤ k := by linarith
  have hgap : k^3 + 3 * k^2 ≤ r^3 := by
    calc
      k^3 + 3 * k^2 ≤ (k + 1)^3 := by nlinarith
      _ ≤ r^3 := by gcongr
  have hp := mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hrhi (show 0 ≤ 12 * n^3 by positivity)
  have hm := mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right hguard hkn
  nlinarith only [hgap, hp, hm, hk]

#print axioms cubic_slack
#check Filling.amplitudeAbs_lt_top_and_cut_le
#check Filling.hasSum_amplitudeSum_change_of_weight
#check Filling.amplitudeSum_besselS_one
#print axioms Filling.amplitudeAbs_lt_top_and_cut_le

end RHProofEditorCutBudget
```

Applying this uniqueness to G(wF(x))−G(w)F(x) proves extended coordinates land in the bicommutant. Finite-coordinate approximation then separates a nonzero denominator. Monic division in the p-complete polynomial presentation gives p^M a=r+z^M w and the exact p^k y=s+zu statement; monic cancellation modulo p proves z-adic separation in the localized ring, without assuming the bicommutant complete or Noetherian.

For the fixed-field coefficient step, Ax–Sen–Tate in the closed-subgroup form is precisely applicable: K=Q_p, H the closed pointwise fixer of the increasing algebraic fields theta phi^(-n)(S)[1/p]. Its fixed subfield of C_p is their completed union. Benois, An introduction to p-adic Hodge theory, Theorem1.1.8(iii), printed p.6, states this form; the lead independently inspected that statement and its page image at the [author's source](https://www.math.u-bordeaux.fr/~dbenoua/Documents/p-adic-Hodge-final.pdf). Characteristic zero removes the purely inseparable extension. The source coordinate retraction clears a denominator in each sufficiently large theta_n image, giving the eventual coefficient equality, rather than a single finite-stage approximation. Chart existence, the integral Amax filtration/reduction, adic basis and Weierstrass hypotheses retain their own proofs/audits. This corroborates and expands the earlier chart notes; it is not an independent audit of the full crystalline frame or a replacement by ordinary rational crystalline admissibility.

### Global theta functional and its absolute normalized identity (26 November)

The [global theta-functional derivation](integration/2026-11-26_COMPACT_GLOBAL_THETA_FUNCTIONAL.md) supplies the existence/normalization input retained by the preceding theta-space and moving-vacuum arguments. Use the actual adelic Heisenberg reproducing Banach model, vacuum e, Haar measure and rational lattice Gamma from the SAME source frame. The rational product formula gives beta(Gamma,Gamma)=1; standard trace-character duality gives all quotient characters as chi_gamma(x)=beta(x,gamma)². The square removes the Heisenberg half. The ordinary annihilator calculation is included: clear the finite component into the codifferent, identify the archimedean trace-dual lattice, subtract that rational vector, then use finite-adelic rational density and local additive nondegeneracy.

Reuse RUN54's Wiener sampling estimate to obtain sum_gamma |<w,rho(gamma)v>|<=C_w||v|| BEFORE defining a theta functional. Thus ell_w(v)=sum_gamma <w,rho(gamma)v> is bounded and rationally invariant. These functionals cannot all vanish: setting v=w=rho(−x)e would make the uniformly absolutely convergent Fourier series sum_gamma e(gamma)chi_gamma(x) vanish, although its mean is e(0)=1. Normalize one nonzero ell by the positive square root of I=mean|ell(rho(x)e)|². Positivity follows from full-support Haar and the vacuum reproducing formula; no nonvanishing L-value is used.

The exact invariant-form normalization has a short proof. Vacuum convolution gives P(v)=integral e(y)rho(y)v=v(0)e. If A is a bounded rho-invariant sesquilinear form, reality/evenness of e gives A(v,e)=A(Pv,e)=conj(v(0))A(e,e); apply this to rho(−x)v and then use reproduction in the second variable. Hence A(v,w)=A(e,e)<v,w>. Apply it to the quotient pairing of two invariant functionals. The normalized theta has mean theta(rho(x)v)conj(theta(rho(x)w))=<w,v>. Its Fourier coefficients are <w,rho(−gamma)v>, absolutely summable by the FIRST sampling estimate. Fourier inversion at zero gives

    sum_(gamma in Gamma) <w,rho(gamma)v>=theta(v)conj(theta(w)).

This is the actual absolute Poisson identity and normalized pairing of ThetaFunctional. It also proves scalar uniqueness of the bounded invariant functional. The identities hold for the already selected normalized source theta, so no independent choice is made at later callers. They supply the global functional in FockThetaFunctions and the existing definite-period/moving-vacuum arguments. The actual vacuum identities, Haar/WeilData construction, real Gaussian action, global pair coherence at the distinct Rallis caller and CM/jet inputs retain their own proofs/audits. This is a human derivation, not newly replayed evidence or the complete RH proof.

### Editor RUN63 — C: construct the actual Kummer chart and remove the coefficient-projection detour (26 November)

Human-derived/source-matched, not native replay. Credit Alpöge, Sernau, Anthropic collaborators and Claude. This section closes the human chart-construction argument retained by literature RUN61, and simplifies its fixed-coefficient proof at the actual period-matrix caller. It does not assert that all Chart foundations, or C itself, have been proved here.

**Chart existence.** Source: [KummerChartExists.lean](https://github.com/lsernau/grh-lean/blob/72fead102f663d5c7a407964a13dc0cf9d977207/grh-compact/src/Lib/PadicHodge/WachKisin/KummerChartExists.lean), blob f90f19a17f77be0aff920a5db18ae389d5898561, Lift.exists_kummer_chart and Lift.exists_smul_teichmuller_eq; HodgeFrame.lean blob 158f4df424f222f593b6f2baee57a294ab221f03, exists_chart_of_pow_mul_eq_neg and Chart.weierstrass_division. Use the native OverGp open image H of Gamma (injectivity is NOT needed here). Open subgroups of the absolute Galois group are closed and correspond to finite extensions K/Q_p, with H=G_K. Write e,f for ramification index and residue degree, so ef=[G_p:H]. The maximal unramified subfield K0 of K has degree f and its integers are the theta-image of Lambda=W(F_(p^f)): the unique prime-to-p Teichmuller roots, fixed by H, identify the residue fields, and p-adic expansion identifies their Witt lifts. Equivalently the native fixes_roots_iff_fixesLambda gives exactly this fixing condition. Choose a uniformizer pi of K and a compatible p-power root tower (pi_n), pi_0=pi. Let z=[pi_flat] in A_inf. It is a nonunit and not p-divisible. The finite local-field facts used here are the unramified/ totally ramified factorization, the Eisenstein uniformizer theorem and compactness of G_K; no general Kisin lattice existence is used.

The (z)-adic completeness of A_inf, used in the native exists_chart_of_pow_mul_eq_neg, supplies the Lambda-algebra evaluation of Lambda[[X]] at z. Under theta this map is ordinary convergent evaluation at pi. Every element of O_K has a convergent expansion in pi with residue representatives from O_K0: repeatedly subtract a lift of its residue and divide by pi. This proves surjectivity of theta on S, the image of the chart series. Choose a unit v in O_K with pi^e v=-p. Lift v by this digit procedure to a series A whose constant term is a unit (v has nonzero residue). Then E=z^e A(z)+p lies in ker theta=(xi). Its mod-p image has valuation e v_p(pi)=1, the same as xi mod p. Thus E=xi u with u mod p a unit of the tilt valuation ring, hence u a unit of A_inf. This is the associated_xi clause, including the correct integral ring, not merely association after inverting p. For e=1 choose pi=-p and A=1; then E=z+p and the stipulated differentExp is zero. In particular this applies when H contains inertia. The chart has the native e and f and fixes theta(z) and Lambda for every g in H. Conversely fixing both implies fixing K0 and pi and hence K=K0(pi), so the chart's fixer is exactly H. Take Gamma'=Gamma, so its index is 1 (coprime to p) and the near-one condition is automatic. The unramified quotient of G_K supplies an element inducing the p^f power on the residue algebraic closure, with no need for a finite-order Frobenius lift.

For each l>=0 the two compatible towers pi_n and eps_n^l pi_n have the same p^n-th power pi. The polynomial X^(p^n)-pi is Eisenstein over K, hence irreducible and separable; the G_K action on its roots is transitive. At each finite n the closed subset of G_K taking the first chosen root to the second is nonempty, and these subsets are nested under p-th powers. Compactness gives a single g for ALL n. Thus g z=[eps_flat]^l z, and a preimage of g in Gamma exists by definition of H. This supplies exactly the native exists_smul_z, not merely independent automorphisms at each finite level. All clauses of exists_kummer_chart are accounted for; the standard period-ring completeness, theta kernel and reduction valuation just invoked remain named foundational inputs.

**A shorter integral image proof at every Frobenius level.** Source: HodgeFrame.lean, Chart.weierstrass_division; ChartScalars.lean blob 8dc59315a323816ecb0eedf46b5aae0a8c714fc5, eventually_exists_coeff_eq_of_fixed and frobE_dvd_iff. The following works for any native Chart, not only the construction above. Put theta_n=theta phi^(-n), pi_n=theta_n(z), d_n=e p^n and K_n=K0(pi_n), with K0 the unramified field above. The identity theta_n(phi^n E)=0 gives v_p(pi_n)=1/d_n because the image of phi^n A is a unit. Here e>0 follows from E being associated to the nonunit xi. Weierstrass division says every s in S has the same theta_n-image as a polynomial of degree <d_n over Lambda. Applied to z^d_n it gives an algebraic relation monic of degree d_n for pi_n over K0. Conversely the valuation denominator forces the ramification index, and thus the degree of K_n/K0, to be at least d_n. The degree is therefore exactly d_n, K_n/K0 is totally ramified, and pi_n is a uniformizer. Repeated residue-digit subtraction in the finite complete field K_n with representatives from O_K0 now proves the exact integral image:
    
    theta_n(S) = O_(K_n),     [K_n:Q_p]=f e p^n.
    
Indeed a digit expansion sum a_i pi_n^i is the theta_n-image of the series with coefficients phi^n(a_i) lifted to Lambda, using that phi restricts to an automorphism of Lambda. Formal truncation errors in z^M become divisible by pi_n^M and tend to zero. The reverse inclusion follows from the polynomial remainder. Furthermore a polynomial of degree <d_n vanishing at pi_n is zero by the degree equality; hence ker(theta_n|S)=(phi^n E)S. This is the exact integral quotient and not just equality of fraction fields.

The fields K_n are nested because pi_(n+1)^p=pi_n. The fixer of S is exactly Gal(Qpbar / union K_n): fixing S fixes every theta_n-image by equivariance; fixing the union fixes every component of the tilt defining z and the Witt coefficient ring, so fixes every convergent chart series. Apply the exact Ax–Sen–Tate theorem already matched by literature RUN61 (Benois Thm 1.1.8(iii), arbitrary closed subgroup, characteristic zero). For a fixed integral tilt element y and fixed component N, approximate theta_N([y]) by x in the union K_n with |theta_N([y])-x|<|p|. This implies x integral. Choose n0 with x in K_n0; for EVERY n>=n0 the integral image equality now gives s_n in S with theta_n(s_n)=x. Reducing modulo p supplies the native eventual equality of tilt components. No coordinate projection or clearing an x-dependent power of p is needed at this step; the integral-image proof is stronger than the finite-dimensional argument. Keep the actual Weierstrass division, theta/phi equivariance, Witt completeness and Ax hypotheses in the ledger. This does not remove coordinate projections from the other chart/bicommutant consumers.

**Peer integration at the real callers.** Literature [RUN61](specialists/literature_provenance/RUN61_COMPACT_CHART_APPROXIMATION.lean), commit 154f74d852c7b3e4a80811c48adc45d42fc38073, supplies the separate coordinate extension on A_max: extend S-linear maps on A_inf by p-adic continuity using the exact intersection A_inf intersect p^m A_max=(p,xi)^m; density after localization yields uniqueness. The commutator vanishes on the dense integral subring, giving the bicommutant clause. Finite coordinate approximations then extend to the whole B^+, without assuming the bicommutant complete. The monic polynomial reduction A_max/p=(A_inf/(p,xi))[X] proves cancellation by (X-b)^n and z-adic separation by finite degree. Integrate these as the source-matched scalar suppliers to PeriodMatrixBasis.exists_basis and KummerChart.exists_witt_coeff_of_isChartOf; the integral image proof above replaces only the fixed-coefficient substep. The actual contraction, adic-basis construction, integral saturation and foundational A_max intersection/reduction retain their own proof and audit status.

Independent [Kummer/corestriction audit](audit/2026-11-26_COMPACT_KUMMER_CORESTRICTION_CALLER_AUDIT.lean), commit 7229fc7e3aca9b9481639715660edf180446bc92, agrees with the integration quotient argument and supplies a shorter native count: writing F for the split field, S for level primes, D=prod_(v in S) F_v^*/F_v^{*m}, E=O_(F,S)^*, the class-group assumption gives J/H=D/(E/E^m), with E/E^m injecting into D by reciprocity and the root criterion. In the totally complex case |D|<=m^(2|S|+[F:Q]), whereas |E/E^m|=m^(|S|+r2); the Kummer injection into the finite character dual supplies the reverse bound and equality. Keep the complex-place hypothesis for the converse. The audit rejects the positive trivialization sign in kernel RUN29: the native relation rho(g)B(x)=B(gx)+z(g) requires B_U(ux0)=-h(u). Integration already uses this sign. ### Editor RUN64 — C: finish the frame determinant contradiction; integrate actual A/B/C suppliers (26 November)

Human source-matched argument, not a new kernel check. Credit Levent Alpöge, Luke Sernau, Anthropic collaborators and Claude. Source lsernau/grh-lean@72fead102f663d5c7a407964a13dc0cf9d977207, Lean 4.35.0 / Mathlib 56a005855ea17e0b0958f73ec801ae140b0a1679. Mission blob 9f4a2272265e847b3a46422b02d4b37e976bee10. Actual PeriodMatrixFrame.lean blob 348d083320f3e9f59100db1a2f96f5a3f8e92923, PeriodMatrixBasis.lean cd91a46eb5624b88af20a96d0450bdf059994b93 and ChartScalars.lean 8dc59315a323816ecb0eedf46b5aae0a8c714fc5 retrieved. The following fills the determinant step in the exact frame theorem, not an appeal to general Kisin existence.

**Basis and contraction at the same caller.** Literature [RUN62](specialists/literature_provenance/RUN62_COMPACT_PERIOD_BASIS_CONTRACTION.lean), commit 34b19e8c036dd14d65c4a2066e7430c8ba4bd541, supplies the uniform denominator and Frobenius descent: for a family a in A_max with xi^h a=u phi(b) for another family member b and fixed u integral, p^(h+1)a=c_k+tau^(h+1)p^k d_k. The induction uses phi(tau)=xi tau and xi=pX in A_max, then reflection of xi^h divisibility back to A_inf. The exact intersection A_inf intersect p^k A_max=(p,xi)^k and completeness yield p^(h+1)a integral. Applied to the coordinates of U fhat(y)=E^h w with Uy=E^h x integral, it gives one exponent N for every f,x,y, not point-dependent denominators. The S-module P of integral x having a bicommutant witness is p-saturated; z is regular on P/pP. Coordinate separation transfers S-independence to A_inf-independence, giving rank at most d. The regular sequence p,z yields the graded description for the (p,z)-adic filtration; choose lifts of a residue basis, and use completeness of S and separation of P to obtain an actual free finite lattice without assuming P finitely generated. The same basis matrices Q,Z have UZ=E^h Q. Coordinate approximation and the fixed exponent N prove the row-divisibility clause for every delta prime to p by closedness of (delta) in the (p,xi)-adic topology and cancellation of p^N. The native z-adic separation then proves det Q nonzero. See the peer's full proof for the ideal closure and descent; the earlier integral chart image in RUN63 supplies the fixed-coefficient step used below. Retain all named ring facts and hU.hat_stable, not an unproved assertion of exactness of Kisin modules.

**Prime-to-p determinant (new explicit caller derivation, Frame lines 66–109).** Write R=A_inf, S the chart ring, T its bicommutant in B^+, O=R/p=O_flat, and a bar for reduction mod p. All matrices below have size d. The basis conclusion is:
(i) det Q != 0, UZ=E^h Q, Z over T;
(ii) every integral x with Uy=E^h x and y in T^d lies in QS^d;
(iii) if l Q=delta g, p does not divide delta, then delta divides l x for arbitrary B^+-solutions.
Use the native Frobenius relation phi(U)=v U F with F in T and v a unit, and the inverse of phi(E) in T. Applying (ii) columnwise to U [phi(E)^(-1)E]^h F phi(Z)=E^h v^(-1)phi(Q) gives B over S with phi(Q)=v Q B (scalar v). In particular det B mod p is nonzero: otherwise the determinant identity implies by induction that p^k divides det Q for every k, since phi on R is an automorphism fixing p and v a unit. This contradicts separation and det Q !=0.

For each adic coordinate lambda_i:R->S and row j of Q consider the S-row lambda_i(Q_j), entrywise. Their reductions span full rank over Frac(O) in the following precise sense. If t in S^d annihilates all these rows modulo p, then lambda_i((Qt)_j) belongs to pR intersect S=pS for every i,j. The adic basis and mod-p separation imply Qt=p x with x in R^d. Indeed all coordinate values are p-divisible, and the adic expansion has its sum in pR (or use separation in O modulo every power of z). Now U(Zt/p)=E^h x, and Zt/p is in T^d since p is invertible there. By (ii), x=Qs, s in S^d. Injectivity of Q yields t=ps. The native maximal-row determinant lemma, applied to S->O with precisely this implication, selects d coordinate rows forming N over S with det N mod p nonzero. We need not assert their span over Frac(O) directly, since the native lemma uses t over S only. This is the bounded-coordinate determinant step.

Suppose for contradiction det Q mod p=0. The native minimal-support argument over valuation domain O chooses a in ker(Qbar), with a_nu=1 and with g(a)=a whenever g is a ring endomorphism preserving its membership in the kernel. A Galois element fixing S pointwise is S-linear on B^+ and preserves A_max, so commutes with multiplication by Z entries; evaluating at 1 shows gZ=Z. It also fixes E. The native Galois covariance rho gU=U gives Q=rho gQ after applying it to UZ=E^h Q and cancelling E^h. Hence Qbar g(a)=rho_bar g(Qbar a)=0, and minimality forces g(a)=a. The entire vector is fixed by the pointwise fixer of S, exactly the hypothesis in Chart.eventually_exists_coeff_eq_of_fixed, not merely fixed by a chosen finite quotient.

Put K=det Bbar det Nbar !=0 in O. For n>=0 let C_0=I and C_(n+1)=B phi(C_n) over S. Because phi acts as p-th power modulo p, transport of the kernel gives
    Qbar C_nbar a^(p^n)=0,    K det C_nbar divides K^(p^n).
For the second statement, det C_nbar=(det Bbar)^(1+p+...+p^(n-1)); 1+(1+p+...+p^(n-1))<=p^n for p>=2 (at n=0 the empty sum is 0). Equivalently induct directly using K det C_(n+1)bar=K det Bbar (det C_nbar)^p and K det Bbar divides K^p. This avoids any inverse of K or a matrix over O.

As O is the perfection of O_C/p, choose M with coeff_M(K)!=0. RUN63's fixed-coefficient theorem now gives, for every sufficiently large n simultaneously in the finitely many coordinates, s in S^d with coeff_(M+n)(sbar)=coeff_M(a). Set s_nu=1, which preserves the equality. Write l=M+n. Since coeff_l(a^(p^n))=coeff_M(a), the transported kernel identity implies coeff_l(Qbar C_nbar sbar)=0, or equivalently theta_l((Q C_n s)_j) is divisible by p for every j. Apply the exact Chart.exists_frobE_dvd_sub_of_dvd with alpha=p and f=lambda_i: theta_l(lambda_i((Q C_n s)_j)) is divisible by p too. By S-linearity these expressions are the selected rows of N C_n s. Consequently over O_C/p,
    coeff_l(Nbar C_nbar) coeff_l(sbar)=0.
The vector has a coordinate 1, so adjugate multiplication forces the determinant of that coefficient matrix to vanish even in this non-domain. But det(Nbar C_nbar) divides K^(p^n) by the product divisibility above, and applying coeff_l yields the contradiction
    0 = coeff_l(K^(p^n)) = coeff_M(K) != 0.
Thus p does not divide det Q. No limit of kernel vectors, inverse of Frobenius on the coefficient quotient, or integral unit determinant was assumed.

**Integral frame and Galois caller.** Apply (iii) to each row of adj(Q) with delta=det Q to get adj(Q)x divisible entrywise by det Q. Cancellation gives x=Q L_x integral for every B^+-solvable x. Taking U' with UU'=(E tau)^h I yields QL=tau^h I. For U entries choose A_inf approximants modulo mu^h after a common p^N as in the mission's existing inverse construction, so X=p^N(U-mu^h W) integral. With mu=t E tau, X=U p^N(I-t^h U'W). Use the lifting clause on E^h X (multiply the witness by E^h) to obtain X=Q M integral. Then U=Q Z' with Z'=p^(-N)M+(tE)^h L W, and det Q !=0 implies Z'Z=E^h I.
